## Accepted card targets

Accepted through S46: 1,150 cards. Later proposed ranges are not acceptance evidence.

| ID | Primary target | Direct production burden | Advance beyond Stage 05 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `es-06-0001` | `relativiza` | Use relativiza to express how a worsening category qualifies a general improvement claim supported by a falling overall complaint rate. | C1 es-05-0988 reconciles increased complaint totals with a larger denominator. Here relativiza directly links the rising noise-complaint rate to a restriction on the general improvement claim: the falling overall rate does not extend to every category. |
| `es-06-0002` | `desactualiza` | Deny that adding untested material invalidates a test of an explicitly unchanged, separately usable component. | C1 es-05-0994 replaces an outdated notice after a change. Here component identity preserves earlier evidence for the retained text while the added videos remain untested; neither wholesale invalidation nor wholesale transfer follows. |
| `es-06-0003` | `extensivo` | Use extensivo under visible negation to deny extending collective assent to an amended proposal after some participants left. | C1 es-05-0185 withdraws the speaker's endorsement after a material change. Here the record supports an earlier all-party vote and a later amendment, but their combination cannot establish those absent participants' assent to the amended package. |
| `es-06-0004` | `incompatibilidad` | Name incompatibility established between two materials whose separate successful tests used different partners. | C1 es-05-1063 separates completion from quality. Here neither component is defective in isolation: the mismatch between the tested pairings and the delivered pairing explains why complete, individually usable provision fails as a kit. |
| `es-06-0005` | `sincronizar` | Deny a need to synchronize services when a retained intermediate resource connects their nonoverlapping schedules. | C1 es-05-1050 finds a common time among offered options. Here no common time is needed: morning access produces the saved copy that the afternoon activity requires, so an actual dependency survives without simultaneous availability. |
| `es-06-0006` | `achacarlo` | Use achacarlo with visible todo and negation to reject an exclusively selective explanation of improved group performance. | C1 es-05-1026 limits findings to completers because withdrawals are excluded. Here matched earlier and later work establishes progress among remaining learners, so attrition limits the group inference without explaining away all observed improvement. |
| `es-06-0007` | `contrarrestado` | Use contrarrestado to describe an improvement offset by a linked adjustment, with successful follow-up limiting the negative inference. | C1 es-05-0549 withholds attribution when two circumstances change together. Here restoring playback volume while retaining reduced background noise supplies a discriminating follow-up: difficulty under the combined settings does not establish that noise reduction was useless. |
| `es-06-0008` | `se frustra` | Explain with se frustra why an apparently acceptable alternative fails through its own access prerequisite. | C1 es-05-1191 identifies an outstanding permission alongside available personnel. Here the proposed means of avoiding live attendance itself requires live attendance; the favorable responses therefore concern a workaround the actual access condition defeats. |
| `es-06-0009` | `permanencia` | Use permanencia to distinguish continued individual presence from repeated occupancy and repeated ticket names. | C1 es-05-1035 rejects individual continuity from reviews by explicitly different people. Here identifiers appear to match, but transferable tickets break the link between repeated names and actual attendees, even with occupied seats independently confirmed. |
| `es-06-0010` | `disociar` | Use disociar to remove signage as a necessary component of the observed accompanied success without assigning it standalone effectiveness. | C1 es-05-1319 establishes combined effectiveness when neither support works alone. Here success without signs establishes that accompanied arrival does not require them, while the missing unaccompanied trial leaves their independent usefulness unresolved. |
| `es-06-0011` | `ha rebajado` | Attribute a lower overall processing time to a changed mix while preserving unchanged performance within both case types. | C1 es-05-0982 contrasts an average with unequal individual experiences. Here a shift toward the faster case type explains an average's movement even though neither type improved; the task separates composition change from processing change. |
| `es-06-0012` | `restituye` | Deny that later complete information restores an earlier opportunity for responsive participation. | C1 es-05-1074 distinguishes compensation from completion of a past service. Here access timing controls which contribution was possible: a transcript supplies the missing answer only after the participant could have used it to question the speaker. |
| `es-06-0013` | `acumulables` | Use acumulables under negation to reject adding savings measured over the same overlapping work interval. | C1 es-05-1015 distinguishes repeated entries from distinct visitors. Here two department-level improvements share the same saved interval, and the customer's total confirms why apparently separate time savings cannot be added. |
| `es-06-0014` | `reproducen` | Explain that successful tests do not reproduce the reported failure condition, leaving the report unresolved. | C1 es-05-1185 questions whether users can detect a loss that occurs. Here the testing procedure omits the condition associated with the reported failure; a negative test does not test the competing claim in the first place. |
| `es-06-0015` | `escalonaron` | Use escalonaron to synthesize an access success achieved by sequencing supports that competed for the same viewer's attention. | C1 es-05-0434 requires turn-taking because one device cannot serve two rooms at once. Here both supports are available, but their simultaneous delivery prevents one recipient from using them; the documented successful sequence changes the access conclusion. |
| `es-06-0016` | `renovación` | Name partial recipient turnover while keeping increased delivery volume and unchanged household coverage distinct. | C1 es-05-1015 separates service events from distinct people. Here new recipients and departing recipients interact with a stable household total: genuine entry into the service coexists with replacement rather than net expansion. |
| `es-06-0017` | `Prescindieron` | Use prescindieron with de to identify which support was removed while retaining dependence on a substitute source. | C1 es-05-0524 withholds autonomous skill from attendance alone. Here a successful repeat attempt apparently without the teacher still copies a preserved solution, so removal of the personal prompt does not remove the instructional dependency. |
| `es-06-0018` | `se redistribuiría` | Project redistribution of contingent requests instead of treating alternative waiting lists as independent demand. | C1 es-05-1015 removes duplicate records of completed events. Here the requests are alternatives whose continued validity depends on the other request remaining unfilled, so providing one option changes demand for the other. |
| `es-06-0019` | `equiparables` | Question comparability of before-and-after waits when a procedural change also moves the start of measurement. | C1 es-05-0959 compares sources with different groups and criteria; es-05-1000 distinguishes durations with and without stops. Here the service change alters its own measurement boundary, allowing shorter recorded waits alongside a longer complete visit. |
| `es-06-0020` | `convalidan` | Deny that favorable responses validate a written version when reviewers had an additional information channel. | C1 es-05-1060 withholds superiority when reviewers know only one option. Here nominally shared exposure conceals unequal content: audio listeners receive a step absent from the subtitles, so their praise cannot validate the subtitle-only explanation. |
| `es-06-0021` | `acotan` | Use acotan to express a bounded attendance count from an exhaustive list and separate lower bounds on early and late arrivals. | C1 es-05-0996 distinguishes a partial list from exhaustive attendance. Here list coverage is complete, but timing is partial; the early observation supplies a floor and confirmed late arrivals lower the ceiling without supplying an exact early count. |
| `es-06-0022` | `da un vuelco` | Use dar un vuelco to express reversal of a service ranking after comparison within both case types. | C1 es-05-0959 withholds a ranking from incomparable evaluations. Here actual within-type comparisons support the opposite ranking from the combined totals because the faster module handles a harder mix; merely withholding comparison would lose a supported conclusion. |
| `es-06-0023` | `echa por tierra` | Use echar por tierra under negation to retain demonstrated understanding despite increased disclosure of doubts. | C1 es-05-1185 considers missing reports when problems may escape detection. Here willingness to report increases while a separate comprehension check improves, so increased questions cannot by themselves overturn the independently observed improvement. |
| `es-06-0024` | `fragmentado` | Use fragmentado to explain why sufficient total capacity cannot accommodate an explicitly linked attendance request. | C1 es-05-1295 rejects simultaneous work when there is only one room. Here total capacity equals total demand, but the places lie in different sessions and the applicants must travel together; allocation, not total shortage, defeats this particular request. |
| `es-06-0025` | `se infiere` | Withhold a jointly qualified access inference when separate records do not link reservation status, time, and stroller use. | C1 es-05-1032 rejects universal access through a counterexample. Here the sources do not establish even one jointly qualified case, yet also provide no counterexample proving such access impossible; the missing link limits the positive inference. |
| `es-06-0026` | `Rastreemos` | Request the history linking additional repair visits to faults previously declared resolved. | C1 es-05-1015 separates duplicate entries from distinct people. This requests recurrence after apparent resolution, which distinguishes additional demand from work generated by unsuccessful repairs. |
| `es-06-0027` | `Desaparecería` | Ask whether the apparent advantage disappears when work from both periods is assessed with one grading criterion. | C1 es-05-0980 identifies different category boundaries. This requests a comparison that holds the boundary constant to distinguish improved work from easier classification before reconsidering support. |
| `es-06-0028` | `Crucen` | Request a person-to-slot comparison separating full usable sessions from sessions outside each family's availability. | C1 es-05-1050 finds a known common time. This asks for the missing match between individual availability and occupied slots, distinguishing insufficient capacity at usable times from unusable scheduling. |
| `es-06-0029` | `Cabía` | Ask whether the response mechanism allowed rejection of the venue rather than merely choosing a date there. | C1 es-05-1041 identifies an omitted option. This tests whether a recorded choice could express rejection of the premise now being treated as endorsed, limiting its use to justify another venue commitment. |
| `es-06-0030` | `se atribuía` | Ask which speaker received credit when an idea passed from an interrupted speaker to someone who finished it. | C1 es-05-1654 corrects overrepresentation caused by repeated turns. This asks whether turn boundaries transfer ownership of an idea, affecting whether individual participation or the attribution process needs attention. |
| `es-06-0031` | `ejecutarlo` | Ask for performance without simultaneous verbal explanation to distinguish procedural difficulty from difficulty explaining it. | C1 es-05-0968 separates evaluation components. This requests an observation that removes one response demand while retaining the procedure, so the two possible difficulties lead to different instructional support. |
| `es-06-0032` | `Se rezagan` | Ask whether rising throughput coexists with selective delay of older complex requests. | C1 es-05-0986 recognizes carried-over work. This requests the interaction of age, complexity, and progress elsewhere in the queue, distinguishing selective delay from a general capacity problem. |
| `es-06-0033` | `se corregían` | Ask which correct final responses emerged during remedial pauses rather than preceding them. | C1 es-05-1038 identifies a known observer intervention. This requests response changes across the proposed support removal, testing whether the success cited for removing it arose during that support. |
| `es-06-0034` | `Te desorganiza` | Ask whether rescheduling itself is the grievance even when the meeting eventually takes place. | C1 es-05-0992 distinguishes cancellation from its stated motive. This elicits a burden that completed-meeting totals cannot answer and tests whether later confirmation would address or aggravate the actual complaint. |
| `es-06-0035` | `objetar` | Ask whether contributors could challenge excerpt selection and seek removal of their own material. | C1 es-05-0980 locates competing definitions, and es-05-1127 asserts an author's right to consultation. This asks whether actual contributors had an effective way to contest representation before treating additional contributions as the remedy. |
| `es-06-0036` | `ponderaron` | Ask how assessment components contributed to the overall result and which prevents progression. | C1 es-05-0968 lists component evaluations. This requests their weight and consequence for placement, distinguishing a global threshold failure from a difficulty that could receive targeted support. |
| `es-06-0037` | `Disponían` | Ask whether early leavers had a feasible return journey if they stayed to the end. | C1 es-05-1039 withholds a specific interpretation of an ambiguous evaluation. This requests whether the rejected option was practically available, testing the inference from departure to dissatisfaction with duration. |
| `es-06-0038` | `Reclasificaron` | Ask whether a reduced backlog reflects a changed classification rule rather than completed remedies. | C1 es-05-0998 separates known process stages. This tests whether the rule assigning cases to a completed category changed, potentially producing the improvement used to justify less follow-up. |
| `es-06-0039` | `ha reconocido` | Ask for the learner's own error recognition before exposure to automatic suggestions. | C1 es-05-1063 separates completion from quality; S01 es-06-0017 identifies a visible model dependency. This asks for decisions made before assistance to determine whether polished output supplies evidence of the skill needed without that assistance. |
| `es-06-0040` | `se inclinarían` | Elicit a format preference with the perceived burden of justifying one option removed. | C1 es-05-1055 reports known inhibited criticism. This asks for a choice under equalized conditions to test whether nonselection reflects format preference or avoidance of an additional burden. |
| `es-06-0041` | `retroceder` | Request the earlier transfer omitted from a clip before interpreting the visible taking of material. | C1 es-05-1022 reconstructs an established custody sequence. This requests the missing beginning that determines the role of the visible action in an interaction and bears on the proposed separation. |
| `es-06-0042` | `Las delatan` | Ask whether textual details reveal respondents' identities without the identifying reader's prior knowledge. | C1 es-05-0812 describes an unnamed source. This asks what combination of published details and audience knowledge enabled identification, rather than equating removed names with anonymity for another audience. |
| `es-06-0043` | `Contabilizaron` | Ask whether a surplus measure combines unserved food with food left on plates. | C1 es-05-0991 separates a gross amount from a known deduction. This questions whether one surplus category combines different points of failed distribution, affecting whether reduced production or different portions merits consideration. |
| `es-06-0044` | `costearlas` | Ask whether current income can cover tables once free provision ends. | C1 es-05-0991 explains an already known expense. This requests replacement of a disappearing noncash contribution in the comparison, testing whether a past operating balance applies to the proposed renewal. |
| `es-06-0045` | `se apartó` | Ask where a wrong solution first diverged from the task, separating operation selection from calculation. | C1 es-05-0968 separates assessment components. This requests the point where an error enters a dependent sequence: later incorrect output can follow an earlier interpretation error even when the calculation itself is sound. |
| `es-06-0046` | `Se desincronizaron` | Ask whether editing altered the timing relation used to judge the choir's entrance. | C1 es-05-0990 distinguishes an event from its unnoticed occurrence. This questions whether the record preserves the relation between cue and response, so an apparent performance defect may instead belong to the recording. |
| `es-06-0047` | `la retirarías` | Ask whether offered help survives rejection of the helper's preferred decision. | C1 es-05-0961 identifies explicitly unequal consultation rights. This elicits an unstated contingency on practical help, testing whether the recipient can exercise a different preference while relying on the offer. |
| `es-06-0048` | `se recuperaron` | Ask how many complaints reached the service by phone after a failed web submission. | C1 es-05-1185 questions whether missing reports establish an absence of problems. This requests linkage across failed and successful channels to determine whether the supposedly dispensable phone service compensates for the form's failures. |
| `es-06-0049` | `sucederse` | Ask which setup tasks require another task's completed output and therefore cannot overlap. | C1 es-05-1000 distinguishes included timing components; S01 es-06-0013 establishes overlapping savings. This requests the still-unknown dependency order that determines whether parallel work can actually shorten the booking. |
| `es-06-0050` | `se arrastraría` | Ask whether extending one task's deadline also shifts the deadlines of tasks that require its completion. | C1 es-05-0950 separates participation mode from obligation. This asks whether a local concession propagates through dependent deadlines, determining whether nominal flexibility remains usable across the course. |
| `es-06-0051` | `se derrumba` | Defend independently documented closure when the original message becomes unavailable, without preserving its allegation as independently verified. | Unlike es-05-1021, the relay carries both a dependent claim and a separately produced observation. Losing the original message does not remove that second evidential route; unlike es-05-1045, no source has withdrawn the causal claim. |
| `es-06-0052` | `se supedita` | Make the missed-connection conclusion conditional on an unverified timetable despite independent verification of the first departure. | Beyond es-05-1030, apparently separate evidence for both premises includes an independently measured departure but a shared timetable. The target retains the conclusion's dependence on the unverified premise, rather than merely separating an event and an unrelated supposition. |
| `es-06-0053` | `empalmar` | Refuse to combine a corroborated handover with a later sighting whose linking object identity depends on the original narrator. | Beyond es-05-1022 and es-05-1029, a second observation does not simply supply another leg of a chain: linking the visible object to the independently received folder requires the very source whose complete route is being checked. |
| `es-06-0054` | `desechemos` | Defend the independence of a prompted inspection while acknowledging that the forecast was copied. | Beyond es-05-1021, prior exposure to the shared report prompts an actual check rather than supplying its result. The target rejects treating that observation as mere repetition while leaving the copied duration dependent. |
| `es-06-0055` | `se retroalimenta` | Expose circular strengthening of an identification while preserving an independent witness to the action. | Beyond es-05-1029, the shared identification returns to its originator as supposed confirmation and increases her certainty. The target challenges that feedback, while the visitor's own observation remains usable for the removal. |
| `es-06-0056` | `la incrimina` | Deny that independent confirmation of a defect supports blame when that confirmation places the defect before the alleged intervention. | Beyond es-05-1030 and es-05-1045, the corroborating copy also supplies a temporal constraint adverse to the copied accusation. Confirming the omission therefore changes the force of the evidence against the named editor, not just its source count. |
| `es-06-0057` | `acredito` | Limit the evidential endorsement of a supervisor's signature to her own participation, excluding the test result she merely relayed. | Beyond es-05-1029 and es-05-1337, a supervisor's own repeated report is being treated as an independent check because of her role. The target explicitly assigns her signature's support to the installation instead of validating the whole notice. |
| `es-06-0058` | `suscribo` | Refuse an endorsement manufactured by extracting assent to witnessed words and removing the limit on attributed intention. | Beyond es-05-1030 and es-05-1057, the proposed excerpt would turn a qualified independent witness into a second source for the original interpretation. The target rejects that specific endorsement while retaining the independently heard quotation. |
| `es-06-0059` | `me exculpa` | Deny that an independently acknowledged action verifies the favorable qualification offered in the same repeated defense. | Beyond es-05-0894 and es-05-1030, an admission about one's own role gives independent support to an account without independently establishing its self-exonerating timing claim. The target evaluates the admission's limited force as a defense and leaves actual lateness unresolved. |
| `es-06-0060` | `se despeja` | Resolve doubt about the obstruction's causal role through an independent intervention while retaining uncertainty about the reported duration. | Beyond es-05-1045, the new observer sees the obstruction removed and the same attempted passage succeed, providing support for the mechanism itself. The target locates the remaining uncertainty in the duration carried by dependent retellings. |
| `es-06-0061` | `se duplicaría` | Distinguish additional corroboration from additional departures when an independent count overlaps a relayed total. | Beyond es-05-1015 and es-06-0021, the independent observation improves support for a subset without adding people to the total. The target rejects counting evidential redundancy as new events while preserving dependence for the remainder. |
| `es-06-0062` | `se multiplican` | Reject treating independent evidence of one promise as several guarantees of its fulfillment. | Beyond es-05-1021 and es-05-1030, independence concerns the recording of the speech event, while the underlying undertaking still belongs to one promisor. The target prevents multiplying assurances by counting independent listeners. |
| `es-06-0063` | `blindar` | Prevent independently witnessed agreement from protecting a group-satisfaction claim based on the same selected responses. | Beyond es-05-1026 and es-05-1030, two apparently separate evaluators share the original selection of responses even though one has firsthand access to the vote. The target denies transferring that independent access into immunity for the shared evaluation. |
| `es-06-0064` | `zanjaría` | Challenge the use of a creator's independent identification to settle a permission dispute involving a different alleged interlocutor. | Beyond es-05-1029 and es-05-1030, the proposed confirmation is directed at the owner rather than the person said to have granted permission. The target exposes that mismatch while retaining independently confirmed use and an unresolved authorization dispute. |
| `es-06-0065` | `rompe` | Reject disqualifying an entire mixed account and identify where its own observation breaks reciprocal reporting dependence. | Beyond es-05-1029, reciprocal reporting is being used to exclude a third account wholesale because it repeats one component. The target establishes the specific component for which that mixed account supplies an additional observation. |
| `es-06-0066` | `descansa` | Locate the remaining dependence of a contents-at-arrival inference after independent observation verifies sealed transport. | Beyond es-05-1022 and es-05-1030, independently corroborated continuity supports a conditional inference whose initial contents still come from one source. The target identifies that necessary starting premise instead of merely distinguishing exterior and interior. |
| `es-06-0067` | `se mantiene en pie` | Preserve a pre-existing independent identification despite later adoption of another observer's interpretation. | Beyond es-05-1029, contact with the original observer raises a contamination objection, but a photo sent before that contact preserves independent support for identity. The target answers that objection without defending the borrowed interpretation of haste. |
| `es-06-0068` | `desmentido` | Reject recasting selective corroboration as an independent denial of the unwitnessed remainder. | Beyond es-05-1029 and es-05-1030, the summary reverses the force of the witness's access limit: lack of corroboration becomes alleged counterevidence. The target corrects that reversal while preserving the independently witnessed diversion. |
| `es-06-0069` | `tergiversaría` | Reject using an independent record to support a duration that conflicts with the announcement's explicit uncertainty. | Beyond es-05-1025 and es-05-1030, the additional record independently verifies the announced change and its stated qualification. Treating it as support for permanence would alter the source's own commitment, not merely increase confidence in an unsupported inference. |
| `es-06-0070` | `generalizada` | Reject both a universal failure claim and blanket dismissal of the original user after an independent test has mixed results. | Beyond es-05-1037, es-05-1053 and es-06-0014, an actual new test reproduces one reported failure while succeeding on the other function. The target limits the general inference without treating the different user's success as a refutation of the original failure. |
| `es-06-0071` | `te fíes` | Withhold independent evidential weight from a printed caption while accepting the photograph's own visual evidence. | Beyond es-05-0892 and es-05-1021, an authentic independently produced image contains a later annotation copied from the original narrator. The target distinguishes trust in the visible place from apparent documentary confirmation of the inherited date. |
| `es-06-0072` | `me excedería` | Limit a witness's endorsement of an evaluative complaint when independent observation covers nonarrival but not the communication that could qualify it. | Beyond es-05-1029 and es-05-1061, the inaccessible notice is consequential for calling the observed absence unexplained nonperformance. The target limits that classification rather than merely listing two separately sourced negative claims. |
| `es-06-0073` | `invalidaría` | Challenge using independent confirmation of a time-limited preparation to discredit a later outcome report. | Beyond es-05-1022 and es-05-1037, the independent source is the person who made a qualified reservation. Her confirmation establishes that limited preparation, which neither guarantees a seat later nor supplies counterevidence to the singly sourced outcome. |
| `es-06-0074` | `desestimemos` | Distinguish an observer's affiliation from actual evidential dependence within a mixed report. | Beyond es-05-0887 and es-05-1021, affiliation is incorrectly used to disqualify a recipient's own count, while her apparent completeness check really does depend on the sender's list. The target defends the observation without treating personal involvement as proof of independence for everything reported. |
| `es-06-0075` | `sigo dependiendo` | Locate the remaining source dependence inside an apparently independent comparison with a replacement session. | Beyond es-05-1057 and es-05-1060, the new observer actually compares the session with a reference, but that reference's completeness comes from the original claimant. The target preserves independent support for the observed session without presenting the comparison as independent verification of full replacement. |
| `es-06-0076` | `compaginarlos` | Identify why scheduling alone cannot reconcile two accepted uses when one requires a continuing physical state that defeats the other. | Beyond es-05-1648, both arrangements are already accepted separately, and preserving one party's condition defeats the other's use; restoring a reservation does not produce a compatible shared arrangement. |
| `es-06-0077` | `te releva` | Reject transferring a downstream recipient's release to the independent return obligation owed to the original lender. | Beyond es-05-0156 and es-05-1412, cancellation removes one actual undertaking in a linked sequence while the upstream obligation survives; the issue is which relationship a release changes. |
| `es-06-0078` | `prorratear` | Reject splitting a single matching contribution when that split changes the terms on which each group accepted its own contribution. | Beyond es-05-2247 and es-06-0018, the overlap concerns a promised resource whose redistribution would alter both reciprocal funding arrangements, not merely duplicate demand or extend permission. |
| `es-06-0079` | `se transfieren` | Reject rerouting reciprocal services to new beneficiaries merely because an intermediary participates in both existing exchanges. | Beyond es-05-1696 and es-05-2255, the proposed shortcut changes the counterpart receiving and returning each favor; willingness under two existing exchanges does not establish the replacement exchange. |
| `es-06-0080` | `desbloquea` | Recognize that releasing a reservation activates another party’s accepted conditional loan without endorsing that party’s activity. | Unlike es-05-1184 and es-05-2036, no reciprocal confirmation loop remains: an actual withdrawal satisfies the condition of a separately accepted loan. Beyond es-05-0978’s distinction between procedure and preference, the target identifies how one party’s withdrawal activates another relationship’s commitment despite explicit nonendorsement. Unlike es-06-0084, the activation condition has occurred; unlike es-06-0100, confirmation does not require or imply a newly accepted all-party alternative. |
| `es-06-0081` | `se cancela` | Preserve an explicitly independent commitment when a failed upstream condition removes the basis for a different linked undertaking. | Beyond es-05-1714, the cue requires following a failed condition through the food commitments while stopping that consequence at an expressly independent resource loan. |
| `es-06-0082` | `anteponer` | Reject treating a downstream group's confirmed schedule as an agreed priority over a conflicting earlier resource commitment. | Beyond es-05-1648, identifying incompatible conditions is insufficient: the target rejects an unsupported priority rule imported from a separate agreement with other participants. |
| `es-06-0083` | `fragmentarlo` | Reject assembling a new arrangement from favorable fragments of two accepted exchanges while discarding an exchange's required return. | Beyond es-05-1412 and es-05-1665, the task concerns two actual accepted packages whose selected components cannot retain their original acceptance after recombination. |
| `es-06-0084` | `se reactivaría` | Deny automatic revival of a released fallback when the agreed activation condition has not occurred and the counterpart has relinquished capacity. | Beyond es-05-1336 and es-05-2255, the fallback had an explicit relation to a primary commitment and a later release; changing preference neither triggers it nor restores the counterpart's reservation. |
| `es-06-0085` | `se prolonga` | Limit a negotiated extension to the relationship that granted it when performance depends on a separately borrowed resource. | Beyond es-05-1855 and es-06-0050, an actual downstream extension leaves the upstream resource deadline unchanged; the speaker must preserve both commitments without claiming that the concession propagates. |
| `es-06-0086` | `fusionarlos` | Reject merging separately accepted mediated conversations when direct joint interaction changes their agreed disclosure relationship. | Beyond es-05-2084 and es-05-1645, both parties have accepted the mediator's involvement; the unresolved consent concerns replacing bounded relay with mutual direct access. |
| `es-06-0087` | `subordinarlo` | Correct reversal of an explicitly one-way dependency while preserving the affected families' conditional participation. | Beyond es-05-0726 and es-05-1714, the target distinguishes the direction of linked commitments: the dependent support cannot be turned into a prerequisite for the independently confirmed activity. |
| `es-06-0088` | `se repartiría` | Reject assigning an organizer's separately accepted shortfall to families whose conditional contributions were capped. | Beyond es-05-1669 and es-05-2255, the organizer has made a separate commitment whose shortfall is being imported into the families' bounded pledges through an assumed distribution. |
| `es-06-0089` | `se restablece` | Limit reversal of a later swap to that swap instead of treating it as automatic restoration of an earlier arrangement involving another participant. | Beyond es-05-1614, two successive accepted exchanges create distinct current obligations; undoing the second does not undo the first or restore every original assignment. |
| `es-06-0090` | `se cumple` | Deny that performing a collective promise of thanks fulfills a participant's separate promise of repair in return for a loan. | Beyond es-05-1412 and es-05-1714, a valid collective undertaking coexists with a personal exchange but cannot discharge it; the target preserves the distinct form and bearer of the return owed. |
| `es-06-0091` | `se compensa` | Reject treating a favor waived for one counterpart as an offset against the speaker's still-pending obligation to another. | Beyond es-05-1696, the speaker tries to settle a reciprocal obligation through a waiver made in another relationship. Forgiving Luis's help neither performs the agreed help for Vera nor establishes her acceptance of a substitute. |
| `es-06-0092` | `nos comprometería` | Identify how a collective endorsement would change a jointly accepted arrangement for presenting explicitly separate and opposing positions. | Beyond es-05-0978 and es-05-1664, the common publication itself is accepted on reciprocal attribution terms; the proposed cover statement would replace those terms with cross-endorsement. |
| `es-06-0093` | `impediría` | Express how fulfilling the installation agreement would prevent fulfilling the restoration promised in the separately accepted trial. | Beyond es-05-2052 and es-05-1162, the target relates two actual commitments: year-long occupation by benches would prevent the return promised after a one-month trial. It tests the conflict between performances, rather than labeling a trial reversible. |
| `es-06-0094` | `se generalice` | Withhold collective acceptance of extending an exception when the existing participants accepted a complementary mix of money and food. | Beyond es-05-0156 and es-05-1665, the exception is part of an interdependent contribution arrangement; generalizing it removes the funding structure under which the remaining participants accepted. |
| `es-06-0095` | `dirimir` | Identify an unresolved priority conflict between two mutually granted decision powers without denying the underlying shared-use agreement. | Beyond es-05-0978 and es-05-1648, each party has accepted the other's bounded authority, but their exercise overlaps and no accepted rule resolves the overlap. |
| `es-06-0096` | `rebasaría` | Reject treating a shared cumulative limit as a fresh allowance for each separately accepted activity, even when their schedules do not overlap. | Beyond es-05-2012 and es-05-2247, the agreements share one expressly global burden ceiling; separate scheduling avoids simultaneous use but cannot make their combined execution satisfy that ceiling. |
| `es-06-0097` | `unifica` | Deny that shared project naming merges enrollment commitments when one team's transfer promise conflicts with the other team's reserved places. | Beyond es-05-2247, the common label overlays two operational commitments; treating it as one enrollment system would transfer a mobility promise into places reserved under the opposite condition. |
| `es-06-0098` | `se diluye` | Expose how assigning transport collectively conceals the absence of a primary undertaking when each participant accepted only the other's backup role. | Beyond es-05-1714 and es-05-1669, the connecting task has two accepted undertakings concerning it, but both are substitutions for an absent counterpart. Neither assigns the ordinary trip when both people are present; the collective wording hides that mismatch. |
| `es-06-0099` | `intercambiarlos` | Reject treating two people who can drive as interchangeable within an agreement whose language support depends on one person's explicitly limited passenger role. | Beyond es-05-1855 and es-05-1685, the accepted division joins two abilities with an explicit role restriction. Driving competence does not preserve the agreed call support under a swap, and no replacement arrangement has been accepted. |
| `es-06-0100` | `deshacerlo` | Defend an expressly confirmed shared alternative from blanket cancellation prompted by incompatibility between the earlier linked offers. | Beyond es-05-1665 and es-06-0003, the cue establishes subsequent all-party acceptance of a bounded alternative; the target preserves that actual agreement without reviving the incompatible financing package. |
| `es-06-0101` | `falsearía` | Reject rewriting a recorded intervention while withdrawing its use as support for a downstream consensus claim. | Beyond es-05-0098, the correction changes the evidential use of another person's conclusion without changing what the original speaker knew or said. |
| `es-06-0102` | `se propaga` | Carry a corrected service restriction into the affected branch of two differently scoped retellings. | Beyond es-05-1311, correction follows transformed content selectively rather than treating all recipients or retellings as equally mistaken. |
| `es-06-0103` | `se bifurca` | Mark divergence when a source withdraws an identification but a former repeater maintains it with a claimed new basis. | Beyond es-05-0909, the correction creates distinct current positions; the downstream speaker's claimed independent evidence remains unexamined. |
| `es-06-0104` | `reasignar` | Reattribute quoted permission while removing the authority claim derived from the mistaken speaker identity. | Beyond es-05-0811, correcting attribution changes whether a downstream account documents authorization; it does not establish the owner's opposing position. |
| `es-06-0105` | `rectifiques` | Require correction of a repeater's added guarantee separately from the source's withdrawn estimate. | Beyond es-05-0811 and es-05-1311, the source correction cannot absorb responsibility for certainty introduced downstream. |
| `es-06-0106` | `se alinea` | Update the reported relationship between accounts when a source correction removes their apparent contradiction. | Beyond es-05-0811, repairing quoted wording also requires revising the downstream portrayal of an independent witness as contradictory. |
| `es-06-0107` | `se deshace` | Undo a synthesized shared judgment when one contributor corrects the object of her evaluation. | Beyond es-05-1045, the correction dissolves the relation that licensed a joint attribution rather than merely removing one causal explanation. |
| `es-06-0108` | `se disipa` | Remove a corrected factual disagreement from the explanation of a continuing practical disagreement. | Beyond es-05-1045, the corrected sources converge on price while their preferences remain opposed, requiring revision of the relationship reported between them. |
| `es-06-0109` | `se desvincula` | Remove a policy attribution from a son's published account while preserving the witnessed refusal and the employee's stated explanation. | Beyond es-05-1045, the corrected explanation remains attributable to the employee, but his action no longer supports the downstream claim that the library requires reservations. |
| `es-06-0110` | `se reclasifica` | Change a downstream event report to unconfirmed planned status while preserving the stronger claim previously published. | Beyond es-05-0417, the correction changes factual status across linked reports without retroactively weakening the repeater's earlier assertion. |
| `es-06-0111` | `restituyamos` | Restore an omitted permission condition within the advice derived from the defective announcement. | Beyond es-05-1311, correcting the source quotation alone leaves a separately worded downstream recommendation overbroad. |
| `es-06-0112` | `des por enterada` | Reject attributing knowledge of a source correction to a downstream speaker who had not received it. | Beyond es-05-1311, the correction's transmission history limits how a later repetition may be characterized, not merely who should receive a notice. |
| `es-06-0113` | `Reformularé` | Reformulate a retained recommendation around newly obtained evidence after its attributed guarantee is corrected. | Beyond es-05-1305, the replacement support was acquired after the correction and cannot be attributed to the source or inserted into the original rationale. |
| `es-06-0114` | `recalculemos` | Recalculate a derived proportion when a corrected total interacts with an independently established subgroup count. | Beyond es-05-1305, the correction changes the relationship between retained and revised quantities; the characterization derived from the original figures no longer fits the corrected total. |
| `es-06-0115` | `resucita` | Identify revival of a withdrawn claim when an obsolete derivative returns as supposed confirmation. | Beyond es-05-1311 and Stage 06 source-dependence tasks, the correction fails to reach an echo that is then used to undo the withdrawal. |
| `es-06-0116` | `se equipara` | Prevent a limited source correction from becoming a downstream claim that the source confessed to fabrication. | Beyond es-05-0909, the retraction itself becomes misreported evidence for a new accusation, requiring correction at that additional attribution level. |
| `es-06-0117` | `se desmorona` | Withdraw a reported change over time when correction removes the earlier state required for the comparison. | Beyond es-05-1318, the correction removes evidence that the alleged transition occurred while preserving independent evidence for its supposed endpoint. |
| `es-06-0118` | `se traspasa` | Block transferring an inferred motive to a newly identified person through mechanical name correction. | Beyond es-05-0909 and es-05-1308, correcting the source's actor does not license reassignment of an accusation added by the downstream writer. |
| `es-06-0119` | `se extiende` | Ask how far a corrected source detail reaches into a mixed personal account whose dependence remains partly unknown. | Beyond es-05-0529, the corrected date may remove support for a downstream event claim rather than merely change its label; that reach cannot yet be settled. |
| `es-06-0120` | `revalida` | Reject automatic restoration of a broad invitation after correction removes the counterexample that prompted its withdrawal. | Beyond es-05-0417, the correction reverses the status of a reported objection without restoring the unsupported universal claim challenged through it. |
| `es-06-0121` | `desarticula` | Dismantle a downstream causal explanation contradicted by the source's corrected event order. | Beyond es-05-1045, the revised sequence supplies evidence against the derived cause rather than simply withdrawing the source's causal confidence. |
| `es-06-0122` | `contradecirla` | Dispute a source's attempt to blame a faithful reporter while accepting that her factual correction removes support for the published claim. | Beyond es-05-0811, the original message supports the reporter's accuracy while its factual content loses support; accepting the correction does not require accepting the source's revised account of who introduced the error. |
| `es-06-0123` | `enmienden` | Carry a service correction to a new recipient's derived plan even though that message omits the erroneous source statement. | Beyond es-05-1311, correcting original recipients or searching for repeated wording misses a transformed practical consequence sent to a different audience. |
| `es-06-0124` | `acumulativa` | Combine successive corrections with different scopes while preserving the valid change already carried into a derivative notice. | Beyond es-05-1311 and es-05-1318, a second correction modifies the updated account without canceling the first correction or restoring the original version. |
| `es-06-0125` | `desdecirnos` | Retract a published claim of double verification after a source corrects her claimed firsthand count, without withdrawing the total or her separate damage observation. | Beyond es-05-1045 and Stage 06 S03, identifying the remaining sources is insufficient: the writers must withdraw their own circulated corroboration claim even though the reported total still has support. |
| `es-06-0126` | `Concedo` | Concede a concrete objection to diminished credit while offering contribution wording that leaves authorship disputed. | Beyond es-05-0980 and es-05-1650, the concession repairs an objection to the proposed credit itself without accepting either authorship boundary. |

| `es-06-0129` | `me atribuyas` | Refuse collective ownership of a disputed classification while offering attributed wording for a shared exhibition. | Beyond es-05-1112 and es-06-0092, the response negotiates how a contested title may appear as one interpretation alongside the dissenter's reservation. |
| `es-06-0130` | `Dejemos en suspenso` | Propose suspending a disputed category total while retaining separately comparable activity descriptions. | Unlike es-05-1147 and es-05-1757, the shared record does not impose one counting definition; its useful comparisons survive without a common study total. |

| `es-06-0208` | `Contrapondría` | Weigh the cited ending against one's interpretation after an ally converts the textual objection into a dislike of happy endings. | Beyond C1 1199/1250, concede the supported reading challenged by the source instead of defending a genre preference assigned by an ally. |
