| `es-06-0210` | `lo concedo` | Concede the lost alternative caused by an unreliable confirmation after an ally reduces the complaint to disappointment at absence. | Beyond C1 1250/1266, preserve the causal object of the complaint and accept the consequential conduct erased by the sympathetic retelling. |
| `es-06-0211` | `se tambalea` | Concede a defective consultation basis rather than rebut a universal attendance prediction that the objector never made. | Beyond C1 1174/1263/2239, the response concerns representativeness of the consultation, not refutation of an original or revised quantified attendance claim. |
| `es-06-0212` | `conciliaría` | Answer the experiential point of a bounded analogy with conversation plus a summary after an ally attacks an invented institutional equivalence. | Beyond C1 1245, preserve the analogy's actual relation and answer its substantive objection rather than reject the distorted comparison. |
| `es-06-0213` | `nos ahorra` | Reject a character defense and distinguish an effective separate backup from a nominal substitute that leaves the original hypothetical loss unanswered. | Beyond C1 1172/1636/2061, answer the stated shared-loss contingency after it is personalized, without either predicting a loss or treating a named substitute as sufficient. |
| `es-06-0214` | `Reexaminaría` | Revise the checking arrangement after an ally turns an objection allowing accidental error into an allegation of deception. | Beyond C1 1199/1264, preserve the source's concession about intent and answer the verification defect instead of supplying a character defense. |
| `es-06-0215` | `atendería` | Defend one offered alternative as a complete answer to the objection after an intermediary combines alternative remedies into a supposedly excessive joint demand. | Beyond C1 1260/1521/1665, correct how the objector's alternatives were combined and show why accepting one answers her objection without accepting the invented package. |
| `es-06-0216` | `desatendería` | Reject a dimensional demonstration aimed at an erased concession and require a use test that addresses the original workspace objection. | Beyond C1 1250/1363 and S02 diagnostic clarification, restore the distinction the objector already stated and choose what a substantive demonstration must test. |
| `es-06-0217` | `despoja` | Defend an altered wording by showing that it retains the reproach actually at issue, after an ally turns the objection into a ban on rewording. | Beyond C1 irony recognition and 1275, evaluate a concrete alternative against the source's pragmatic objection while rejecting the invented requirement of verbatim copying. |
| `es-06-0218` | `nos obliga` | Accept the required change to a public justification after an intermediary recasts disputed sponsorship as opposition to the project. | Beyond C1 1267/1274, preserve the source's open evaluation of merit and answer the false claimed mandate rather than defend usefulness. |
| `es-06-0219` | `lo rebate` | Refute the actual width comparison with measurements instead of defending competence against the intermediary's personalized version. | Beyond C1 1199/1247, supply evidence that defeats the source's specific premise while repairing the shift to an alleged judgment of expertise. |
| `es-06-0220` | `desmonta` | Defeat the claimed omission in the objector's copy by locating its instructions, rather than reject an invented inspection demand. | Beyond C1 1241/1257, answer the supported scope of the actual complaint with copy-specific evidence instead of responding to inflated verification requirements. |
| `es-06-0221` | `me exime` | Retain the warranted part of a complaint when the original message limits a promise but still covers the missed call, rather than defend against an invented employment demand. | Beyond C1 bounded promises and 2108, correct both the intermediary's demand and the complaint's universal scope without using that correction to erase the supported breach. |
| `es-06-0222` | `disiparía` | Answer a conditional shared-source reservation with earlier separate drafts instead of demanding proof of an asserted copying allegation. | Beyond C1 1250 and S03 provenance, preserve conditional force and use the supplied chronology to answer only the dependency actually raised. |
| `es-06-0223` | `vulneraría` | Defend participation records that do not disclose choices after an intermediary turns a request for secret voting into a ban on checking who voted. | Beyond C1 1646 and permission-scope mediation, answer the alleged conflict with duplicate-vote control by preserving the precise secrecy sought in the original objection. |
| `es-06-0224` | `legitimaría` | Reject retrospective justification of disclosure while preserving the forgiveness an intermediary erases, and answer the objection to how that forgiveness is being used. | Beyond C1 1385/1509/1607, the response concerns misuse of an accepted apology as evidence about the original act, not another apology or renewed relationship consent. |
| `es-06-0225` | `subsana` | Reject a majority result as an answer to the restricted choice of topics while preserving the adult authority the intermediary claims is being challenged. | Beyond C1 1183/1267 and S02 0029, answer the objection to who supplied the options without defending adult control over a separate decision the source already accepted. |
| `es-06-0226` | `nos quita` | Remove a corrected duration from opposing evaluations while preserving their differently weighted remaining grounds. | Beyond C1 1283/1305, the admitted error removes both an ancillary benefit and the rival's main objection; acting and soundtrack judgments remain separately supported. |
| `es-06-0227` | `rescatar` | Block preservation of a rival interpretation through a narrower description that does not undo the conceded event. | Beyond C1 1308/1177, acknowledging an overlooked encounter defeats both an omission complaint and praise of permanent separation; silence cannot selectively restore the latter. |
| `es-06-0228` | `nos ata` | Reject treating one's reopened venue choice as commitment to the rival option after a shared scarcity premise fails. | Beyond C1 1284/1170, reinstating the center also removes the rival room's claimed exclusivity; separate equipment and space considerations survive. |
| `es-06-0229` | `rebasarían` | Withdraw an overflow consequence from the rival scheduling argument after conceding one's own capacity error. | Beyond C1 1164/1305, correcting duplicate entries removes the necessity claimed for two competing remedies, while preserving distinct lighting and schedule-access reasons. |
| `es-06-0230` | `reencauzar` | Redirect a rival personnel remedy when the conceded error is located upstream of both proposed interventions. | Beyond C1 1045/1297, the correction defeats both retraining and replacement as remedies for these omissions without erasing independent training and workload needs. |
| `es-06-0231` | `atribuirte` | Reject retaining a benevolent intention after withdrawing its hostile interpretation when the shared deliberate-act premise fails. | Beyond C1 1308, accidental omission defeats both opposed intention claims while leaving the experienced exclusion and independently organized meal open to separate evaluation. |
| `es-06-0232` | `se esfuma` | Remove present irreplaceability from the rival's objection without promising that replacements will remain available. | Beyond C1 1312/1315, correcting uniqueness removes the buyer's exclusivity claim and the rival's present replacement objection, but leaves future availability unresolved. |
| `es-06-0233` | `exigiría` | Apply a conceded absence of obligation to the rival's proposed attendance demand without rejecting the offered session. | Beyond C1 1165/1243 and Stage06 S04, correcting one's coercion claim also removes the opponent's warrant for compulsory attendance, leaving availability and personal conflict distinct. |
| `es-06-0234` | `se libra` | Apply a withdrawn access guarantee to a rival backup while retaining the distinct failure that backup addresses. | Beyond C1 1172/1282 and Stage06 S07, one's concession also limits the competing backup's coverage without denying either search usefulness or protection against a single device failure. |
| `es-06-0235` | `recalcularíamos` | Recompute the rival alternative's cost after withdrawing one's own sunk-payment argument. | Beyond C1 1173/1305, the concession removes pressure to retain one booking and improves the competing option's cost comparison, without deciding between content and schedule preferences. |
| `es-06-0236` | `graduemos` | Adjust both instructional proposals to uneven preparation after conceding the shared assumption of universal competence. | Beyond C1 1281/1310, the correction requires different levels of guidance and challenge across two competing teaching proposals; it preserves learner agency and the useful instrumental distribution. |
| `es-06-0237` | `pospongamos` | Remove an incorrect deadline as grounds for both cutting content and postponing, without assuming the remaining work fits. | Beyond C1 1293/1318, the concession removes a shared urgency rationale from competing schedules while leaving completion time unresolved and preserving the identified layout and text defects. |
| `es-06-0238` | `Consultémosles` | Request an answerable consultation after conceding that a blocked channel cannot support either shortening messages or closing the group. | Beyond C1 1185/1277, the response replaces both policies' unusable silence evidence with direct questions, while keeping existing wording requests and access needs separate. |
| `es-06-0239` | `equipararnos` | Reject an equal-loss verdict while accepting that both arguments misused the same popularity count. | Beyond C1 1190/1315, correcting the shared metric removes both mass-approval and crowd-following claims without making personal enjoyment equivalent to a specific textual criticism. |
| `es-06-0240` | `recalibrar` | Revise the rival's waste comparison after conceding an unfounded single-use limit, preserving the unresolved durability issue. | Beyond C1 1305/1252, confirmed washing changes the consumption assumption in both recommendations without converting washability into proven longevity. |
| `es-06-0241` | `se desinfla` | Withdraw the rival's multi-person coordination inference together with one's own claimed independent agreement. | Beyond C1 1177 and Stage06 S03, corrected authorship has reciprocal argumentative effects: neither independent consensus nor coordination among distinct critics survives, while textual merits remain discussable. |
| `es-06-0242` | `circunscribiría` | Limit the rival's contribution base when conceding an overstated universal demand claim. | Beyond C1 2239/1307, the counterevidence reduces both a spatial demand estimate and a competing funding base while leaving observed parking pressure and cleaning needs intact. |
| `es-06-0243` | `extraigamos` | Block a new guarantee of companionship after conceding that an unrepresentative silence supported both quietness and isolation judgments. | Beyond C1 1283/1177, the correction reaches both opposed uses of the same observation and blocks the rival's reversal from unsupported isolation to guaranteed companionship. |
| `es-06-0244` | `concederte` | Refuse a rival exclusion as the supposed consequence of conceding a false novice status. | Beyond C1 1284/1177, proof of experience simultaneously defeats a first-opportunity rationale and novice-based exclusion, leaving observed teaching quality and an actual scheduling obstacle. |
| `es-06-0245` | `reorganicemos` | Separate the responses to two learner groups after conceding the absence premise behind replacement and closure proposals. | Beyond C1 0416/1281, new beginners require revising both an advanced-only replacement and a closure rationale; demand survives even where staffing may require postponement. |
| `es-06-0246` | `separaría` | Reopen whether to travel together before choosing a common destination after conceding a nonexistent booking restriction. | Beyond C1 1170/1258 and Stage06 S04, the correction removes both parties' claimed authority to impose their destination and restores a separate choice about traveling together. |
| `es-06-0247` | `sustentemos` | Adopt the rival's verified alternative justification while conceding that neither cancellation nor retention can rest on repeated content. | Beyond C1 1305/1298, the speaker helps rebuild the rival's defense around actual question resolution after the shared repetition premise fails, without conceding the disputed duration. |
| `es-06-0248` | `te respalda` | Remove the rival's ability-based exclusion warrant after conceding that a random allocation cannot support one's own selection. | Beyond C1 1177/1306, the corrected allocation mechanism defeats both superior-ability endorsement and dominance-based exclusion while preserving observed reliability and limited time. |
| `es-06-0249` | `justificaría` | Limit the rival's refusal to the overnight visit wrongly inferred in one's own proposal. | Beyond C1 1258/1280 and Stage06 S04, retracting an invented overnight invitation removes the rival room-use objection to a daytime visit while leaving travel fatigue and the desire to meet intact. |
| `es-06-0250` | `depuraría` | Remove a defective attendance premise from competing redesign and closure proposals without asserting the opposite turnout. | Beyond C1 1185/1316, one's correction invalidates both low-appeal and inefficient-space-use arguments based on the same count, preserving legibility evidence and competing room requests. |
| `es-06-0251` | `te escudes` | Reject treating a combined answer as complete when it assumes the fact one included question disputes. | Beyond C1 0556, the permitted shared response contains a dependency: answering the motive does not resolve the disputed event on which that answer rests. |
| `es-06-0252` | `Desdoblemos` | Separate represented voices without requiring separate performers when a permitted casting choice has obscured simultaneous disagreement. | Beyond C1 0346, the shared performer changes how conflicting positions are attributed; the correction preserves the casting exception while restoring the relation between voices. |
| `es-06-0253` | `Delimitemos` | Locate the established failure in the missing basis for choosing a special order, without inventing its delivery deadline. | Beyond C1 1263, the delivery exception activates a different decision basis; its omission both supports the information complaint and prevents a deadline-based verdict. |
| `es-06-0254` | `nos compromete` | Recognize that protecting the original activates the substitute needed to fulfill the promised tactile experience. | Beyond C1 1263 and 0599, the excluded contact is precisely what makes the compensating access commitment applicable. |
| `es-06-0255` | `ampliemos` | Preserve the unresolved ending while withdrawing a supposedly available interpretation excluded by an explicit clue. | Beyond C1 0346 and 2239, absence of a stated solution does not preserve every proposed possibility; the counterexample changes the available interpretations without requiring narrative closure. |
| `es-06-0256` | `suprime` | Correct the access route without letting a loan exception erase the consultation option the catalog must identify. | Beyond C1 1258 and 1263, the proposed correction would turn a restriction on how access occurs into an exclusion from access information itself. |
| `es-06-0257` | `se extienda` | Limit a format exception when the exempt passage also carries a promised narrative instruction. | Beyond C1 1263, the same sung passage belongs to an exempt format and a covered communicative function; the correction can preserve the song exemption. |
| `es-06-0258` | `reiteraría` | Renew the feedback request under the deadline shifted by the permitted late submission. | Beyond C1 0570 and 0599, the exception changes the starting point of a surviving obligation rather than merely preserving another duty. |
| `es-06-0259` | `Desestimaríamos` | Reject retroactive acceptance as evidence that an availability exception preserved the promised opportunity to choose. | Beyond C1 0568 and 1259, the failed consultation cannot be repaired merely by obtaining the same answer after the choice has already been made. |
| `es-06-0260` | `Deslindemos` | Acknowledge missing verification while keeping public identification unavailable as a remedy and leaving truth unresolved. | Beyond C1 0812, the protected evidence boundary constrains the response to an actual verification gap; anonymity neither fills that gap nor licenses disclosure. |
| `es-06-0261` | `Suspendería` | Defer the response complaint while rejecting a shifted receipt date that would change the promised queue position. | Beyond C1 0599, the permitted response delay cannot become a new receipt date because that date governs a separate ordering commitment. |
| `es-06-0262` | `penalicemos` | Preserve the chosen deformation while applying the matching criterion to the shape it actually creates. | Beyond C1 0357 and 1273, the permitted artistic choice determines the content of the surviving criterion; the correction must follow that choice rather than revoke it. |
| `es-06-0263` | `nos excusa` | Distinguish an allowed repeated hesitation from a repeated modal whose separate scope must survive editing. | Beyond C1 0815, identical repeated wording has different protective scope within one proposition and across two; the objection warrants restoring only the independently scoped doubt. |
| `es-06-0264` | `Reevaluemos` | Trace the effect of substantive help into the learner's subsequent explanation while preserving permitted transcription. | Beyond C1 0547 and 0593, the unprotected contribution affects later learner-authored wording; deleting the helper's own words does not delimit its influence. |
| `es-06-0265` | `se cierre` | Keep assessment open when the offered alternative falls after the cutoff, without certifying unshown learning. | Beyond C1 0524 and 0570, the provider's timing makes the protected alternative unusable for the promised outcome; the response preserves eligibility without dispensing with assessment. |
| `es-06-0266` | `desborda` | Preserve freedom to recolor while restoring the contrast that carries the promised team distinction. | Beyond C1 0346, the permitted visual feature also encodes a covered distinction; preserving that function requires contrast, not literal color fidelity. |
| `es-06-0267` | `Rebajaría` | Revise the recommendation where missing advance notice prevented an informed choice about a legitimate noise exception. | Beyond C1 1263 and 1310, the notice was the stated means of accommodating the exception; its omission weakens that accommodation without refuting quietness outside it. |
| `es-06-0268` | `restituiría` | Restore a deleted change instruction when familiar wording incorrectly places a changed operation inside the omission exception. | Beyond C1 1263, the exception cannot be classified by the unchanged label: the operation covered by the promise has changed beneath it. |
| `es-06-0269` | `Rotulemos` | Correct the status of an imagined addition within a figure while retaining both the permitted reconstruction and the surviving evidence. | Beyond C1 0346 and 1258, a single composite figure crosses the qualification boundary; labeling or rejecting it as a whole would erase the distinction promised to viewers. |
| `es-06-0270` | `salteada` | Describe discontinuous reading so a truthful endpoint does not conceal gaps relevant to a recommendation. | Beyond C1 0348 and 0560, the permitted nonsequential route makes endpoint disclosure insufficient; the repair preserves the reading freedom while changing how its scope is reported. |
| `es-06-0271` | `repondría` | Restore shared authorship without identifying the protected contributor. | Beyond C1 0568 and 0812, omitting one identity changes the displayed relation between contributors if it is treated as absence of a contribution. |
| `es-06-0272` | `reclamemos` | Reject blanket identification and an anonymity-based dismissal of an admitted duplicate while leaving the corrective method unresolved. | Beyond Stage06 0223's preventive participation check and 0200's uncertain authorship, the duplicate is admitted after anonymous submission; a necessary correction remains constrained by the protected procedure. |
| `es-06-0273` | `Reubicaría` | Apply the consultation queue when an exempt pickup changes into a consultation during the same visit. | Beyond C1 0561 and 1263, the qualification changes during one encounter; earlier priority remains legitimate without extending to the newly requested service. |
| `es-06-0274` | `Insistiríamos` | Request a local functional correction to the very feature protected as handmade variation. | Beyond C1 0357 and 0556, the allowed spacing itself causes the binding failure; the correction limits that variation at the affected point without standardizing the whole finish. |
| `es-06-0275` | `Puntualicemos` | Acknowledge the missing orientation triggered by a permitted navigation change while leaving function removal unproved. | Beyond C1 1263, the promised notice should make the allowed change navigable; its omission supports that access complaint without establishing disappearance. |
| `es-06-0276` | `Convengamos` | Propose reachable seated workspaces as the count for placing materials, with a recount if the reach remedy removes seats. | Beyond 05-1181 and 06-0216, negotiate a test whose access criterion changes the count supporting a separate materials decision. |
| `es-06-0277` | `equiparemos` | Negotiate a comparison that distinguishes a usable individual position from lighting suitable for both readers. | Beyond 05-1234 and 1283, specify how a shared comparison can direct either placement changes or lamp selection while retaining one person's usable setup. |
| `es-06-0278` | `se considere` | Propose distinct evidential uses for an unaided first attempt and performance after a hint, preserving the order needed to inform advancement and guided practice. | Beyond 05-0547 and 06-0033, negotiate an observation order that lets one trial inform two teaching decisions; giving the hint first prevents that same attempt from establishing unaided performance. |
| `es-06-0279` | `diferenciemos` | Negotiate separate uses of unmet requests and choices made while both dishes remain available. | Beyond 05-1195 and 06-0190, define when the same serving trial informs quantity and when changing availability prevents interpreting choices as comparative preference. |
| `es-06-0280` | `desdoblaría` | Propose establishing the interpretation behind laughter before evaluating the effect; clarification may remove the basis for retaining the pause. | Beyond 05-1275 and 1303, negotiate how an interpretation check changes the relevance of an observed comic effect without settling whether that effect is desirable. |
| `es-06-0281` | `se contabilicen` | Negotiate counting failed searches as time consumed while using those same failures to identify missing entries. | Beyond 05-1285 and 1305, reject excluding observations useful for content repair from the separate service-time criterion; repair also changes the version being timed. |
| `es-06-0282` | `condicionaría` | Condition the recovery check on retaining its uncorrected result, allowing operational correction without treating it as recovery evidence. | Beyond 05-1185 and 06-0201, negotiate how repairing the working list would erase the observation needed for the separate backup decision. |
| `es-06-0283` | `descontaría` | Refuse to exclude offsite work from the workload criterion while accepting room release as a different scheduling endpoint. | Beyond 05-1208 and 06-0182, negotiate distinct endpoints when moving tasks improves room availability without reducing the work being allocated. |
| `es-06-0284` | `vinculemos` | Propose attributing shared help to each arrival while counting its location and duration as one intervention. | Beyond 06-0010 and 0188, negotiate how one-to-many assistance informs sign placement and staffing without equating beneficiaries, questions, and interventions. |
| `es-06-0285` | `me conformaría` | Reject closure as the shared endpoint and propose checking whether one person's retrieval preserves the other's ready-to-use arrangement. | Beyond 05-1258 and 06-0216, negotiate a completion criterion for interdependent uses: retrieving one person's item can undo the other person's tested arrangement. |
| `es-06-0286` | `Sopesaría` | Propose comparing speed for correct readings while retaining errors as a separate selection constraint. | Beyond 05-1292 and 1303, negotiate which observations enter a speed comparison so erroneous speed cannot improve it, without assuming agreement on the remaining speed-error tradeoff. |
| `es-06-0287` | `nos bastaría` | Propose different checks for retaining a coordinated team and selecting a solo demonstrator, preserving the role allocation under test. | Beyond 05-1220 and 06-0031, negotiate a trial that preserves the tested interaction between assigned roles while separately assessing a possible individual replacement. |
| `es-06-0288` | `Rescatemos` | Recover the first emptying record for storage and pickup questions without treating cumulative throughput as simultaneous capacity. | Beyond 05-1303 and 06-0178, negotiate distinct uses of a trial whose repeated emptying enables throughput while preventing a test of storage until closing. |
| `es-06-0289` | `Admitiríamos` | Propose counting an informed channel choice separately from whether its attempted submission succeeds. | Beyond 05-0530 and 1274, negotiate how a single failed attempt can still express a preference; counting only completed requests would distort both channel selection and functionality review. |
| `es-06-0290` | `punto de corte` | Propose failure as a cutoff for paper selection while leaving production timing unanswered if the material must change. | Beyond 05-1317, negotiate asymmetric stopping consequences: the same failure can settle material rejection while preventing a completion-time estimate. |
| `es-06-0291` | `desliguemos` | Keep a staffing check tied to the version on which volunteers conditioned their offers, preserving the original applause's object. | Beyond 05-0552 and 06-0084, negotiate an availability check when changing the artistic option also changes the condition of the offers needed to stage it. |
| `es-06-0292` | `computemos` | Exclude a scripted objection from the opinion tally while accepting its delivery as evidence about the response channel. | Beyond 05-1165 and 06-0238, negotiate two uses of an actual response whose deliberately supplied content makes it useful for channel testing but unsuitable as a participant's stance. |
| `es-06-0293` | `convirtamos` | Reject the early reminder as a shared deadline for readiness and noncompliance; propose observing both endpoints without prompting early returns. | Beyond 05-1313 and 06-0033, negotiate competing temporal criteria because a readiness intervention prevents the same returns from establishing lateness under the actual deadline. |
| `es-06-0294` | `Haría valer` | Use one person's corrections as evidence about her guidance while excluding them from the learner's own decisions, then propose a role-preserving check. | Beyond 05-0547 and 06-0031, negotiate the different evidential status of the same intervention for two people being considered for different next activities. |
| `es-06-0295` | `sirviera` | Negotiate timely completion as support for the tested activity's duration while requiring a new timing check after expanding creative choices. | Beyond 06-0137, negotiate interacting timing and choice criteria: the change needed to investigate creative freedom also changes the activity whose duration was established. |
| `es-06-0296` | `rechazaríamos` | Defend an interrupted trial's usefulness for revising the setup method while denying that its stopping time measures completion. | Beyond 05-1252 and 1317, negotiate a shared stopping rule whose activation answers the disturbance question but prevents answering the completion-time question. |
| `es-06-0297` | `supeditaría` | Propose counting arrivals independently of seating when a density trial itself changes admission capacity. | Beyond 06-0190 and 0250, negotiate an observation rule that keeps a proposed atmosphere change from suppressing the demand relevant to the competing venue criterion. |
| `es-06-0298` | `se distinga` | Negotiate a check of whose expressed views survived pair summaries without treating omission as absence of initial participation. | Beyond 06-0012 and 0137, use the trial's successive speaking and selection stages to negotiate what supports participation, summary repair, and the still-unsettled final discussion format. |
| `es-06-0299` | `Nos pondríamos de acuerdo` | Seek agreement that reaching a section counts for navigation even if its content fails the query, while retaining immediate lookup evidence. | Beyond 05-0521 and 1288, negotiate a trial that can separate navigation success from content failure when the original successful lookup bypassed navigation entirely. |
| `es-06-0300` | `se dé por probado` | Reject repeating a tied trial until a winner appears; propose using preference reasons to distinguish a correctable problem from an unresolved selection rule. | Beyond 05-1279 and 1315, negotiate when further questioning would support revision and when selection still requires a decision rule that the shared results do not supply. |
| `es-06-0301` | `me desmarco` | Withdraw identification with an alleged shared doubt when its attributed origin is disputed, without asserting that the trip will succeed. | Beyond C1 report versus endorsement, the challenged attribution changes the speaker's own derivative stance and defeats the claimed agreement. |
| `es-06-0302` | `puedo dar fe` | Defend an attribution of doubt about a narrator's sincerity against a denial concerning the embedded accusation. | Beyond C1 0815, the reporter can maintain the challenged attribution because doubt about professed suspicion does not commit Eva to the brother's innocence. |
| `es-06-0303` | `avalo` | Endorse a source's objection to being cited as a witness even while obtaining further support for one's own account. | Beyond C1 0896 and S03, the speaker endorses a challenge to the corroboration claimed for the addressee's recommendation; stronger personal evidence cannot establish Nora as a witness. |
| `es-06-0304` | `sigo dudando` | Withhold belief in an alleged reversal of stance when the source describes increased access to evidence without confirmation. | Beyond C1 0802, the speaker challenges a reported transition between two commitments; changed knowledge supports neither the alleged earlier rejection nor the current endorsement. |
| `es-06-0305` | `doy por refutada` | Maintain rejection of a disproved endorsement despite pressure from the original complainant to withdraw that criticism. | Beyond C1 0794 and S05 attribution repair, renewed commitment to the complaint cannot overturn the speaker's warranted rejection of its alleged endorsement. |
| `es-06-0306` | `suscribiría` | Offer conditional endorsement after the reported agreement loses its basis, preserving the conditions on both attributed and personal commitment. | Beyond C1 0895, the challenge to a reported endorsement requires a new explicit basis for the speaker's conditional alignment. |
| `es-06-0307` | `Me ratifico` | Reaffirm a report of maintained commitment while rejecting an alleged switch from disbelief to belief. | Beyond C1 0790 and S05 0122, the defended claim concerns Pablo's continued commitment as reported by the editor; defending it neither adopts his explanation nor reverses an earlier rebuttal. |
| `es-06-0308` | `Te concedo` | Concede the loss of attributed support without granting the opposing side ownership of that source's judgment. | Beyond C1 scope correction and S10 reciprocal concessions, the concession concerns competing attributions of endorsement; withdrawing one attribution cannot establish its rival. |
| `es-06-0309` | `me pronunciaría` | Condition a judgment about an alleged invented request on resolving its disputed origin after receipt is acknowledged. | Beyond C1 0802, correcting the reported denial settles receipt while leaving authorship open; the speaker must locate the remaining condition on judging the accusation. |
| `es-06-0310` | `doy por sentado` | Refuse to treat a renewed invitation as disproof of a disputed earlier report of cancellation. | Beyond C1 0816, direct confirmation now warrants accepting the invitation while leaving both the earlier cancellation and its contested transmission unsettled. |
| `es-06-0311` | `haya hecho suya` | Deny an inference of full endorsement while retaining the evidential value of a contribution to the disputed argument. | Beyond C1 0791, the challenge to attributed endorsement limits source-based support without removing the speaker's warrant to accept an embedded example. |
| `es-06-0312` | `Tengo por cierto` | Assert certainty about the intermediary's provisional response while withholding certainty about an alleged promise by the original source. | Beyond C1 0813, direct evidence settles the intermediate response and restricts the attributed guarantee without deciding whether the venue will open. |
| `es-06-0313` | `me aventuraría` | Decline to report newfound confidence when a source withdraws an attributed agreement but maintains his own doubt. | Beyond C1 0912, denial by the supposedly aligned source changes the reportable agreement without reversing the intermediary's explicitly maintained commitment. |
| `es-06-0314` | `me haya retractado` | Deny an attributed retraction of one's recommendation when only its claimed shared endorsement was withdrawn. | Beyond C1 0909 and S05 0113, later personal evidence strengthens the recommendation without validating a nested account of two reversals of opinion. |
| `es-06-0315` | `podría sostener` | Withhold endorsement of an alleged earlier confirmation even after the underlying explanation is independently established. | Beyond C1 0896 and S03, later factual support changes the speaker's belief about the cause but cannot overcome a source's documented denial of earlier endorsement. |
| `es-06-0316` | `me comprometería` | Refuse to endorse a portrayal of disagreement created from the author's denial of a supposed endorsement. | Beyond C1 0790, limiting the author's approval changes the speaker's warranted public alignment: lost support cannot be recast as an opposing authorial interpretation. |
| `es-06-0317` | `he cuestionado` | Maintain a challenge to alleged eyewitness authority without accepting an attributed admission that one's remembered account was false. | Beyond C1 0909, the witness's clarification supports the speaker's actual objection while defeating a further report that transfers that objection to the remembered content. |
| `es-06-0318` | `se haya desdicho` | Reject an inferred retraction by the original speaker when only another person's authorization is denied. | Beyond C1 0816, withdrawal is itself the contested embedded claim, and the representative's denial cannot transfer responsibility for retracting the criticism. |
| `es-06-0319` | `lo pongo en duda` | Question the continued applicability of one's earlier objection when an intermediary claims new evidence, without accepting attributed disbelief in the original source. | Beyond S05 0103, the speaker revises what they can maintain about present support while defending the limited past scope of their own challenge. |
| `es-06-0320` | `Haría mía` | Adopt a source's reservation after a challenged attribution of narrative certainty prompts an unsupported opposite ending. | Beyond C1 0905 and conditional adoption, the speaker's stance preserves the consequence of Irene's correction without transferring the daughter's certainty to the narrator or converting it into proven error. |
| `es-06-0321` | `Nos consta` | Assert the scope of a denied verification against an outer report that turns it into a claim of universal incomprehension. | Beyond C1 0813, certainty concerns what the interpreter denied having checked; that commitment warrants challenging the organizers' account without certifying either comprehension or failure. |
| `es-06-0322` | `Puedo responder` | Take responsibility for a temporally bounded report of consideration while rejecting the attributed agreement and an unsupported current-state inference. | Beyond C1 0811, the source's dated denial distinguishes responsibility at two reporting levels without fixing her present position. |
| `es-06-0323` | `Me retractaré` | Offer retraction conditional on evidence of the disputed endorsement while defending the actual report's explicitly limited commitment. | Beyond C1 0816, the retraction request concerns an alleged reporter commitment; its evidential condition must not become a judgment about the original suspicion. |
| `es-06-0324` | `daría crédito` | Tentatively credit an intermediary's recorded understanding while leaving the original wording and the child's stance disputed. | Beyond C1 0807, a source's denial changes what a contemporaneous note can warrant at each level without requiring rejection of the intermediary's account. |
| `es-06-0325` | `habrá que admitir` | Accept decisive evidence of expressed distrust despite its denial while preventing the reporter from treating that distrust as factual refutation. | Beyond C1 0815, the challenge is resolved only at the level of expressed commitment, preserving uncertainty about the explanation and limiting the reporter's conclusion. |
| `es-06-0326` | `haya contribuido` | Allow a learning contribution while distinguishing new-problem performance from results still compatible with memorization. | Beyond 05-0549 and 06-0203, the supplied performance types give the competing explanations different reach within the same improvement. |
| `es-06-0327` | `daría por buena` | Reject unanimity as an explanation of public silence without treating that rejection as proof of personal inhibition. | Beyond 05-0350, private objections defeat one interpretation of the conceded behavior while leaving the speaker's competing mechanism provisional. |
| `es-06-0328` | `refute` | Concede an actual delay without allowing it to erase an overlapping obstacle or absorb the entire delay. | Beyond 05-0232, the accepted obstacle and its rival operated concurrently; acknowledging either changes neither the other's existence nor its temporal reach. |
| `es-06-0329` | `me persuade` | Express a bounded causal belief where the competing explanation no longer applies, while retaining ambiguity during the shared period. | Beyond 05-0549, the speaker's commitment differs between vacation and working periods because the rival mechanism has a stated time limit. |
| `es-06-0330` | `se deba` | Limit a deterioration claim by distinguishing newly disclosed old conflicts from potentially new conflicts. | Beyond 05-0236 and 06-0023, incident dates allocate parts of the conceded increase differently; disclosure cannot explain away every recent problem. |
| `es-06-0331` | `haya desencadenado` | Allow a possible trigger without converting prior tension into either a complete explanation or proof of the trigger. | Beyond 05-0549, the competing claims concern onset and prior tension; conceding the latter cannot settle the former in either direction. |
| `es-06-0332` | `Convengo` | Concede an immediate recruitment route without denying the upstream influence that supplied some of its invitations. | Beyond 05-0549, the mechanisms are linked for identified cases; neither participant may convert acknowledgment of that link into exclusive or universal credit. |
| `es-06-0333` | `haya frenado` | Keep an initial intervention effect possible while assigning later persistence different evidential force after reversal. | Beyond 05-0236 and 06-0198, persistence without the intervention supports the rival for the later period without retrospectively excluding an initial effect. |
| `es-06-0334` | `tomaría partido` | Side with a previously stated explanation where reported reasons support it, without adopting universal or retrospective certainty. | Beyond 05-0350, prior predictions and subsequently supplied reasons give unequal support to competing accounts of the conceded outcome. |
| `es-06-0335` | `haya propiciado` | Allow an initial enabling influence without imposing that causal direction on later voluntary choices. | Beyond 05-0549, assigned proximity and subsequent chosen proximity support opposite causal directions at different stages of the same relationship. |
| `es-06-0336` | `se atribuya` | Reject exclusive credit while acknowledging enabling support without transferring creative agency. | Beyond 05-0549, the concession must preserve who supplied materials and who created the work, limiting both exclusive gratitude and inferred authorship. |
| `es-06-0337` | `haya acelerado` | Concede an established effect on timing while rejecting both invented initial willingness and dismissal of the earlier meeting. | Beyond 05-2114, bounded assent resolves competing claims about causing an event versus changing an existing plan; neither account absorbs the other's supported part. |
| `es-06-0338` | `concedería` | Concede a navigation effect for initial arrivals while withholding independent confirmation from potentially dependent later arrivals. | Beyond 05-0350 and 06-0010, one successful route creates the rival route for later participants, changing what repeated success can corroborate. |
| `es-06-0339` | `haya pesado` | Keep a late personal influence possible while rejecting its extension to an earlier anonymous evaluation. | Beyond 05-0213, revealing identity changes when the proposed influence becomes available; uncertainty about the final choice does not erase the earlier advantage. |
| `es-06-0340` | `se sostenga` | Make a universal attendance explanation conditional on prior awareness while conceding a favorable later reception. | Beyond 05-0350, the disputed inference moves from reception to earlier decisions; it requires information access that the supplied reservation dates leave unresolved. |
| `es-06-0341` | `coincidiría` | Limit agreement about time saved while preserving both reduced interruption time and omitted work. | Beyond 05-0549 and 06-0283, the concession must distinguish two documented causes of shorter sessions because only one supports the claimed improvement in efficiency. |
| `es-06-0342` | `lo descarto` | Keep a reconciliatory effect possible while accepting the occasion that already existed when attendance was refused. | Beyond 05-0350, the prior refusal separates the existence of an invitation from a later change of response without proving the unobserved motive. |
| `es-06-0343` | `avale` | Concede sincere favorable responses without endorsing a general explanation when the evaluated process also controls access to responding. | Beyond 04-0390 and 06-0020, the proposed explanatory property filters whose experience becomes visible; that limitation does not invalidate the observed satisfaction. |
| `es-06-0344` | `demos por cierta` | Reject apparent confirmation of an initial motive when publicizing that claim helped produce the conceded outcome. | Beyond 05-0350, the explanatory claim itself affected attendance; accepting its documented effect cannot establish either initial disinterest or responsibility for every absence. |
| `es-06-0345` | `acreditaría` | Challenge the inference that a successful repair makes the failed substitute route irrelevant. | Beyond 05-0549 and 06-0060, accepting a demonstrated obstacle leaves a preventable failure of an alternative route causally relevant. |
| `es-06-0346` | `haya influido` | Accept a demonstrated influence on appearance without treating it as confirmation or exclusion of material alteration. | Beyond 05-0540 and 06-0277, a reversible perceptual effect warrants causal assent at the observation level while leaving the rival claim about the object unexamined. |
| `es-06-0347` | `suscribamos` | Reject invented joint endorsement while retaining different causal contributions tied to participants' stated barriers. | Beyond 05-0549 and 06-0196, distinct barriers positively support both accounts for different people; factual agreement cannot transfer the speaker's endorsement to an exclusive account. |
| `es-06-0348` | `da pie` | Recognize bounded support for interest from engagement continuing after the competing incentive ended. | Beyond 05-0350, post-incentive discussion changes what the conceded attendance supports for an identifiable subgroup without assigning everyone the same motive. |
| `es-06-0349` | `demostrara` | Reject an effect claim while accepting that the conceded difference instead explains assignment to the support group. | Beyond 05-0549, the same results determine group membership before the intervention; reversing that relation would turn the reason for support into evidence of harm. |
| `es-06-0350` | `inferiría` | Reject a no-attraction inference from capped attendance without converting present excess demand into an established increase. | Beyond 05-0549 and 06-0190, the speaker accepts the ceiling's effect while withholding both competing claims about change because previous unmet demand is unknown. |
| `es-06-0351` | `inevitable` | Reject inevitability as the status of a possible absence while retaining the need for a substitute. | Beyond es-05-0479's limited inference, reject using a necessary precaution as grounds for replacing the scheduled performer before her absence is established. |
| `es-06-0352` | `te exime` | Preserve the duty to offer an invitation when a prediction of refusal is being used to excuse withholding the choice. | Develops es-05-0584's continuing obligation through a disputed prediction: expected refusal concerns the invitee's choice, while the commitment concerns making that choice available. |
| `es-06-0353` | `puedo comprometerme` | Commit to the requested status response while withholding a delivery guarantee that the available information cannot support. | Builds on es-05-0577 by resolving a customer's demand for information and a supplier's mistaken certainty threshold for responding at all. |
| `es-06-0354` | `Me abstendría` | Withhold a disclosure under an actual confidentiality commitment without allowing that restraint to imply ignorance or an unfinished decision. | Develops es-05-0798 and 0802 through a known disclosure restriction whose misreading would falsely announce that the deciding body remains undecided. |
| `es-06-0355` | `tomo partido` | Affirm a personal position while observing a voting restriction that another participant wrongly extends to expressed opinions. | Extends es-05-0571 by repairing the use of a voting restriction to erase an explicitly stated preference from the group's account. |
| `es-06-0356` | `Se exige` | Maintain a requirement to mark unverified information despite one participant's confidence, without presenting the required warning as a finding of falsity. | Builds on es-05-0912 by resolving how a required uncertainty marker interacts with conflicting personal confidence and verification status before publication. |
| `es-06-0357` | `Puede quedarse` | Restore permission to wait while keeping room entry restricted and eventual accommodation uncertain. | Beyond es-05-0455 and 0561, give a permitted interim response when lack of a room guarantee is mistaken for a ban on waiting; that response neither permits room entry nor guarantees accommodation. |
| `es-06-0358` | `estemos obligados` | Reject an asserted duty to cancel when the agreed trigger requires confirmation and the available message expresses only a possibility. | Develops es-05-0445 by correcting an action requirement inferred from weaker evidence than the participants' cancellation condition requires. |
| `es-06-0359` | `desaconsejé` | Deny having advised against a film when an epistemic reservation is being used to replace another participant's required preview. | Builds on es-05-0912 by preventing a reservation from becoming advice against an option and thereby displacing another person's own preview requirement. |
| `es-06-0360` | `cabe exigir` | Reject a demand for reasons that turns the group's uncertainty into an unauthorized condition on an already clear decision to leave. | Extends es-05-0798 through conflicting scopes: an unexplained but definite withdrawal and a group's need to investigate do not create a duty to justify leaving. |
| `es-06-0361` | `te creas obligado` | Preserve freedom to attempt an uncertain project without treating shared doubts or another person's refusal as a shared duty to decline. | Develops es-05-0479 through two people who share an uncertainty but choose differently; neither person's practical choice determines the other's modal commitment. |
| `es-06-0362` | `Es de esperar` | State an expectation about audience questions while limiting the organizers' assigned duty to providing an opportunity to ask. | Builds on es-05-0441 and 0573 by separating an expected audience response from the organizers' assigned preparation, preventing that assignment from becoming audience compulsion. |
| `es-06-0363` | `doy mi palabra` | Promise to respect the owner's response, removing the supposed duty to persuade that is making the prospective tenant give up wanted companionship. | Beyond es-05-0577's limit on control of another person's response, negotiate the companion's role: a commitment to restraint removes an assumed persuasive duty without overriding the tenant's choice to go alone. |
| `es-06-0364` | `exigiríamos` | Identify the additional demand imposed by treating difficulty recognizing a signature as established failure to supply one. | Develops es-05-0802 by exposing the practical burden imposed when a reviewer's uncertainty is treated as an applicant's established omission. |
| `es-06-0365` | `impida` | Reject uncertainty as a bar to excluding a demonstrable translation error, while preserving interpretive choice and the requirement of textual justification. | Beyond es-05-0477 and 0908's limited evidential conclusions, prevent uncertainty about intention from overriding a textual requirement within an expressly permitted choice of translations. |
| `es-06-0366` | `Renunciaría` | Express a personal decision not to travel under uncertainty without turning that decision into certainty of failure or a constraint on a companion. | Extends es-05-0586 through two travelers' different decision thresholds: a personal refusal under uncertainty cannot establish impossibility for the other traveler. |
| `es-06-0367` | `Debemos` | Affirm the agreed obligation to review changes reported by authors while preserving the separate restriction on publishing unsupported accusations. | Beyond es-05-0914's burden of proof, resolve two interacting instructions: the threshold for publishing an accusation cannot be imposed on the review expressly required when an author reports a change. |
| `es-06-0368` | `acataría` | Commit to honoring a stated request without treating compliance as a denial of another person's prediction about the requester's reaction. | Beyond es-05-0561's permission boundaries and 0586's personal preference, honor an explicit request without first disproving someone else's prediction of enjoyment; compliance does not accuse that person of lying. |
| `es-06-0369` | `se impone` | Assert the need for a recount when the governing instruction makes unresolved doubt, rather than proven loss, its trigger. | Extends es-05-0445 by rejecting a proof threshold that would disable an instruction specifically activated by unresolved contradictory counts. |
| `es-06-0370` | `Me veo obligada` | Express a practical need to decline a place to satisfy a request for a definite decision without claiming future attendance is impossible. | Develops es-05-0798 and 0912 through a definite practical answer required before factual availability is known, preserving the difference between declining and predicting impossibility. |
| `es-06-0371` | `Puedo negarme` | Preserve the option to refuse a demonstration without confirming an assessor's doubt or demanding an assignment that requires that demonstration. | Builds on es-05-0569 and 0802 by preserving both voluntary refusal and a separate demonstration requirement for assignment, without treating unverified ability as inability. |
| `es-06-0372` | `Podemos discrepar` | Affirm that differing predictions remain compatible with the authorization expressly given despite those differences. | Beyond es-05-0576's missing consent and S04's interlocking permissions, preserve consent already given under acknowledged doubt: continued pessimism about the result does not require withdrawing the prediction or invalidate that consent. |
| `es-06-0373` | `Puedes interrumpir` | Make the director's permission to pause usable despite a fictional order to remain, without changing the character's obligation when the scene resumes. | Beyond es-05-0748 and es-06-0320's ownership of fictional experience or certainty, resolve a practical conflict between an actor's permitted pause and a character's required waiting; exercising the real permission need not enact fictional disobedience. |
| `es-06-0374` | `Estoy dispuesta` | Offer voluntary participation without lending that willingness to a moderator's proposed requirement that everyone disclose a personal experience. | Beyond es-05-0573's optional participation, answer the moderator's fear of silence with a voluntary contribution that respects the organizer's wish to avoid pressure, without extending the volunteer's willingness to everyone. |
| `es-06-0375` | `quede prohibido` | Reject a proposed prohibition on provisional registration when that permitted process is needed to resolve the uncertainty cited against it. | Develops es-05-0479 through a circular practical restriction: uncertainty about opening cannot prohibit the expressly permitted registration process needed to reach confirmation. |
| `es-06-0376` | `habría ofrecido` | Withdraw the hypothetical lodging offer when the host's revised knowledge removes its premise, despite the guest's stated willingness. | Beyond es-05-0310 and es-06-0084, revise the offer generated by the host's knowledge before evaluating the guest's hypothetical acceptance. |
| `es-06-0377` | `hubieras contado` | Reject warranted reliance on a newly imagined willingness that never reaches the person arranging the work. | Beyond es-05-0096 and es-06-0210, separate revised willingness from the recipient's grounds for relying on it under an unchanged refusal. |
| `es-06-0378` | `habrías conservado` | Preserve the independently offered visit while revising the pickup information generated by a mistaken travel assumption. | Beyond es-05-0296 and es-06-0081, trace which part of a past invitation survives a hypothetical correction of the host's information. |
| `es-06-0379` | `hubieras dado` | Lower the recipient's warranted commitment when revised sender knowledge changes the status attached to an unchanged file. | Beyond es-05-0402 and es-06-0163, revise what the recipient could treat as settled after a counterfactual change in the sender's description. |
| `es-06-0380` | `habría podido escoger` | Restore the customer's choice between the corrected offer and an available substitute without treating the revision as accepted. | Beyond es-05-0290 and es-06-0253, reconstruct the customer's available decision after changing the information that generated the original offer. |
| `es-06-0381` | `habría debido avisarle` | Require notification before reclaiming an assignment when revised knowledge changes the organizer's intention but the substitute already has a confirmation. | Beyond es-05-0091 and es-06-0084, preserve the substitute's received confirmation while revising what the organizer wanted to do. |
| `es-06-0382` | `habrías rechazado` | Leave the recipient's response open when revised requester knowledge changes the size of the request. | Beyond es-05-0098 and es-06-0215, prevent an actual refusal from deciding the response to the different request produced by corrected past knowledge. |
| `es-06-0383` | `hubieras esperado` | Withdraw the inviter's expectation of personal disclosure when corrected knowledge changes the setting covered by the guest's acceptance. | Beyond es-05-0090 and es-06-0086, revise the disclosure an inviter could expect when corrected knowledge would have changed the audience described in the invitation. |
| `es-06-0384` | `hubieras detectado` | Withdraw an expectation of correction when changed sender knowledge removes the recipient's only access to the material. | Beyond es-05-0310 and es-06-0168, revise the review expected from another person when the counterfactual blocks the information on which that review depended. |
| `es-06-0385` | `habrías podido preguntar` | Recover a possible follow-up question when corrected sender knowledge changes a closed decision into a pending one. | Beyond es-05-0430 and es-06-0375, recover a possible follow-up to a hypothetical pending notice instead of carrying over the recipient's response to a definitive cancellation. |
| `es-06-0386` | `rectificara` | Remove the need for an intermediary's correction under an accurate initial announcement while preserving the organizer's separate need for arrival information. | Beyond es-05-0433 and es-06-0167, reconstruct which replies the organizer would still need after revised sender knowledge eliminates an erroneous announcement and its remedial exchange. |
| `es-06-0387` | `habrías podido cambiar` | Condition the recipient's recovered choice on timely delivery, not merely on the sender's hypothetical earlier knowledge. | Beyond es-05-0309 and es-06-0205, distinguish an earlier corrected decision to notify from the information arriving in time to preserve another person's option. |
| `es-06-0388` | `habrías seguido esperando` | Preserve a travel expectation supported by a separate direct confirmation after revising the speaker's unreliable assurance. | Beyond es-05-0296 and es-06-0167, retain the recipient's independently warranted expectation under a counterfactual withdrawal of one information route. |
| `es-06-0389` | `habrías quedado` | Reject transferring a recipient's agreement to a different favor after corrected knowledge eliminates the original request. | Beyond es-05-0317 and es-06-0083, remove the request that licensed a past acceptance without preserving that acceptance as a freely transferable commitment. |
| `es-06-0390` | `hubieran elegido` | Keep individual casting choices unresolved when corrected organizer knowledge requires a consultation absent from the original assignment. | Beyond es-05-0404 and es-06-0374, revise how roles would have been offered and refuse to carry actual silence over as answers to the hypothetical choice. |
| `es-06-0391` | `explicara` | Remove the expected justification when corrected delivery knowledge turns a presumed refusal into an unanswered invitation. | Beyond es-05-0096 and es-06-0195, reconstruct the response the caller could request after changing the past information that made an alleged refusal intelligible. |
| `es-06-0392` | `habría entendido` | Interpret the inquiry generated by corrected guest knowledge as interest in lodging while withholding the stronger inference of a confirmed stay. | Beyond es-05-0402 and es-06-0084, replace a hypothetical refusal with an information request and delimit the host's resulting expectation: interest warrants route advice, not a confirmed reservation. |
| `es-06-0393` | `hubieras garantizado` | Reduce the assurance a recipient could responsibly pass onward when the original sender's revised knowledge weakens the incoming message. | Beyond es-05-0402 and es-06-0157, carry a counterfactual reduction in the first sender's certainty through the assurance the recipient could justifiably pass to a group. |
| `es-06-0394` | `se hubiera sumado` | Reopen a companion's agreement when knowledge-sensitive accommodation changes the feature that motivated that agreement. | Beyond es-05-0106 and es-06-0099, revise a third participant's possible acceptance as a consequence of the speaker's hypothetical accommodation of another participant. |
| `es-06-0395` | `me habría correspondido` | Shift responsibility from the recipient's inquiry to the informed speaker's notification when hypothetical reading resolves the speaker's uncertainty. | Beyond es-05-0433 and es-06-0171, revised access to an existing confirmation reverses the expected communication: the informed speaker should supply the answer instead of sending the recipient to obtain it. |
| `es-06-0396` | `hubieras reclamado` | Remove the basis for addressing a complaint to a named person when corrected sender knowledge changes the criticism's attributed source. | Beyond es-05-0098 and es-06-0309, revise the recipient's warranted interpersonal demand under the counterfactual attribution rather than merely correct source identity. |
| `es-06-0397` | `hubiera rehecho` | Reject the need to recreate completed work when revised requester knowledge would have produced a request to send the existing result. | Beyond es-05-0433 and es-06-0168, change the response required from another person when the requester's hypothetical discovery replaces a request for new work with one for its delivery. |
| `es-06-0398` | `habrías podido esperar` | Revise the adviser's expected influence when the requester would have disclosed an already expired deadline and asked for help within that limit. | Beyond es-05-0309 and es-06-0366, hold the expired deadline fixed while changing the request and the adviser's information, thereby removing an expectation of influencing course selection. |
| `es-06-0399` | `hubiéramos obtenido` | Reopen the expected collective result when corrected membership knowledge gives an omitted participant an actual opportunity to choose. | Beyond es-05-0404 and es-06-0211, reassess the hypothetical agreement when revised membership knowledge gives an omitted participant a choice whose outcome cannot be copied from the actual vote. |
| `es-06-0400` | `hubiera renunciado` | Reassess a third person's reliance on a loan only under an explicit hypothetical decision to withhold it after corrected supply information. | Beyond es-05-0310 and es-06-0152, trace corrected information through a lender's still-open choice to the recipient's reliance, without treating loss of a backup as automatic withdrawal of the offer. |
| `es-06-0401` | `agradezco` | Thank a notifier after correcting the assumption that the notifier knew the recipient was already informed. | Beyond C1 0614 and 0624, the recipient revises the reception of a fulfilled notification undertaking using the notifier's lack of access to a separate private message. |
| `es-06-0402` | `sigo debiendo` | Retain a promised personal contact when shared knowledge of completion does not supply the arrangements the recipient expected. | Beyond C1 0612 and Stage06 0090, separate the notifier's assumption that shared information completed the exchange from the recipient's still-unmet purpose for the promised call. |
| `es-06-0403` | `Asumiré` | Own the correction of an assurance about another person's receipt while preserving the intermediary's separate confirmation commitment. | Beyond C1 0612 and Stage06 0112, a mistaken attribution of receipt induced a second participant to stop checking; responsibility for that assurance and the unfinished check remain separate. |
| `es-06-0404` | `hagamos depender` | Reject making either apology conditional on the other after both participants withheld promised information under reciprocal mistaken assumptions of knowledge. | C1 0633 acknowledges joint responsibility; Stage06 0091 separates debts to different people. Here reciprocal mistaken attributions of full knowledge leave two independent communication failures to acknowledge. |
| `es-06-0405` | `debías comprobar` | Require evidence of final receipt before treating an outcome undertaking as fulfilled through an intermediary assumed to have access. | Beyond C1 0640 and Stage06 0098, the sender's access, the intermediary's unusable link and the intended recipients' receipt remain separate; assumed relay access cannot establish completion of the accepted outcome. |
| `es-06-0406` | `descargues` | Reject transfer of an accepted notification task based on assumed chat readership while allowing a new offer of help. | Beyond Stage06 0098 and 0099, mistaken attribution of access cannot establish a substitute notifier, and correcting that access now does not retroactively establish delegation. |
| `es-06-0407` | `puedo exigir` | Withhold a definitive action demand until the addressee receives the confirmation that only the requester has. | Beyond C1 0629 and Stage06 0358, factual confirmation exists for one participant but remains an unresolved possibility for the person expected to act under a conditional undertaking. |
| `es-06-0408` | `Podía atenerme` | Defend reliance on the recipient's explicit but overbroad report of knowledge when assessing a conditional notification promise. | Beyond C1 0096 and Stage06 0090, distinguish what the recipient actually possessed from the knowledge they reported, which supplied the notifier's basis for treating the condition as unmet. |
| `es-06-0409` | `sigo pidiendo` | Maintain the request to perform a promised message review after withdrawing an attributed refusal based on someone else's reading. | Stage06 0391 removes a demand to explain an unseen invitation's supposed rejection. Here correcting the reader's identity changes the request to the still-unfulfilled review explicitly promised by the addressee. |
| `es-06-0410` | `me disculpo` | Extend responsibility for a false attribution of deadline knowledge to the person who faced the resulting demand. | C1 0877 separates retraction from apology. Here the speaker apologizes to the person subjected to an unsupported deadline after already correcting the intermediary's false belief about that person's knowledge. |
| `es-06-0411` | `descuentes` | Reject shortening an agreed response period by counting time when only another participant had the revised information. | Beyond C1 0106 and Stage06 0387, the mismatch in attributed receipt changes how an actual agreed deadline must be applied, rather than merely mitigating a past delay. |
| `es-06-0412` | `Dejo sin efecto` | Withdraw a requested visit after distinguishing an unmet information need from the unnecessary second verification the addressee inferred. | Beyond Stage06 0090 and 0397, actual evidence of the third party's knowledge resolves competing understandings of a live request, allowing its requester to cancel that specific undertaking. |
| `es-06-0413` | `Suspendamos` | Suspend judgment when an assertion of another's knowledge is downgraded to an assumption without establishing ignorance. | Beyond C1 0627 and Stage06 0309, the uncertain fact is whether an accepted notification undertaking became actionable; neither the earlier assurance nor its withdrawal settles responsibility. |
| `es-06-0414` | `pediré` | Redirect an immediate demand to the informed participant's accepted teaching task before expecting execution from the rest. | Beyond C1 0607 and Stage06 0098, correcting a claim of shared procedural knowledge changes the sequence and addressee of the warranted requests, while retaining the actual explanation commitment. |
| `es-06-0415` | `Seguía obligado` | Defend an unchanged confidentiality undertaking when a listener treats disclosure to both participants as grounds for expecting discussion. | Stage06 0354 separates discretion from ignorance. Here the listener also knows the content and attributes mutual awareness; neither that knowledge nor awareness of it cancels the source's unchanged restriction. |
| `es-06-0416` | `Sobrestimé` | Scale back causal credit based on mistaken assumptions about the audience's ignorance while retaining acknowledgment of a fulfilled task. | Beyond C1 0623, later evidence of the audience's independent knowledge changes the credit warranted for successful arrival, without converting the notifier's uninformed redundancy into failure. |
| `es-06-0417` | `Acoto` | Narrow criticism to the announcement actually supported by the recipient's partial information and accepted assignment. | Beyond C1 0612 and Stage06 0322, an intermediary's broad attribution of being informed cannot enlarge a limited notification task into an obligation to explain details never supplied. |
| `es-06-0418` | `debes responder` | Maintain an accepted receipt response because knowing a location does not tell the sender whether the recipient has it. | Beyond C1 0614 and Stage06 0377, both parties possess the practical information, yet the sender lacks knowledge of the recipient's access; the promised response resolves that specific coordination need. |
| `es-06-0419` | `Completemos` | Request completion of a handover after an acknowledgment of knowing the orders was taken to include their unreceived delivery dates. | C1 0782 preserves pending work through a personnel change. Here a mismatched acknowledgment of knowledge prematurely closes an information handover; the recipient requests the missing part while correcting their own answer's scope. |
| `es-06-0420` | `negociemos` | Reframe an alleged neglected pickup duty as a favor to negotiate after correcting the recipient's assumption about the prospective helper's knowledge. | Stage06 0098 identifies an unassigned task among explicit commitments. Here a mistaken assumption of awareness underlies a personal demand; correcting it opens negotiation without retrospectively accepting the favor. |
| `es-06-0421` | `Reoriento` | Retain criticism of a promised notice while withdrawing consequences falsely attributed to the recipient's supposed ignorance. | Beyond C1 0623 and Stage06 0388, independently received information limits the complaint's causal reach without establishing that the original notifier fulfilled the separate promise. |
| `es-06-0422` | `bastaba` | Limit a relay obligation to the agreed verbatim transmission despite a recipient's mistaken attribution of topic-specific knowledge to the source. | C1 0811 corrects misquotation; Stage06 0322 separates wording from another person's overreading. Here a source's topic-specific knowledge is overestimated, but an explicit verbatim-relay undertaking does not include verifying that knowledge. |

| `es-06-0677` | `Me parodiaba` | Recover the speaker's earlier performed voice for a listener who attached the comic title to her own trip. | Es-05-1494 distinguishes quotation from assertion and es-06-0635 retracts a boast; here an absent listener needs the miniature performed episode to distinguish self-parody from a comment about her. |
| `es-06-0678` | `Deshago` | Repair a service decision caused by hearing a personified callback without access to the staff's earlier game. | Beyond the literal intention repairs in es-05-1498 and 1503, explain the unavailable fictional exchange and distinguish its renewed voice from an actual service limitation. |
| `es-06-0679` | `Omití` | Correct an intermediary's incomplete retelling by restoring the invented participant while leaving the owner's reaction unknown. | Es-05-1495 corrects an exaggerated quantity; this repair corrects missing comic premises without claiming that the restored premise establishes everyone's enjoyment. |
| `es-06-0680` | `les atribuyan` | Relay the omitted rehearsal episode while preventing applause from being reported as an audience's understanding of the callback. | Es-06-0311 separates reconstruction from endorsement; here the repair distinguishes a private comic interpretation of applause from the public audience's actual access and response. |
| `es-06-0681` | `Restablezco` | Repair a listener's interpretation by reordering the supplied setup and punchline, preserving the fictional level. | Es-05-1494 repairs quoted ownership; this listener repairs a reversed relay in which the order of an invented action and its comic consequence determines whether a protest belongs to the performance. |
| `es-06-0682` | `la retiro` | Withdraw the demand that recovering a private reference must make a hurtful welcome enjoyable. | Es-05-1488 and 1509 already acknowledge hurt despite intent; the new repair specifically retracts the equation between receiving missing comic context and accepting the joke. |
| `es-06-0683` | `Actualizo` | Repair an incomplete account of a shared mishap by supplying the discovery that changes how a later callback distributes responsibility. | Es-05-1494 restores quoted ownership and es-06-0635 repairs self-directed irony; this relay corrects a listener's partial episode, whose missing outcome makes a joint comic failure appear to single out one participant. |
| `es-06-0684` | `Reunamos` | Combine two listeners' complementary access to reconstruct the comic transition from an invented musical role to its pretend exhaustion. | Es-05-1548 reconstructs an available practical argument; this repair joins the role assignment known to one listener with the comic continuation known to the other, without adding an unseen motive or episode. |
| `es-06-0685` | `te corresponda` | Explain an inaccessible comic title while preserving a real duty established by a separate, explicit agreement. | Es-05-1503 removes a duty wrongly inferred from permission; this repair prevents recognition of a joking frame from cancelling an independently agreed task. |
| `es-06-0686` | `Te la cuento` | Relay the episode needed to understand a comic toast while withholding the identity that the source excluded from permission to retell it. | Es-05-1497 withholds a private disclosure; this relay supplies the missing comic relation within an explicit disclosure limit, preserving access to the joke without revealing the person's identity. |
| `es-06-0687` | `Se lo describo` | Provide a verbal account of the visual contrast that makes a humorous remark accessible to a listener who cannot see its referents. | Es-05-1492 requests repetition of unheard words; this repair makes the already heard remark recoverable by describing the missing visual relationship, without requiring the listener to see the images or share the laughter. |
| `es-06-0688` | `Entrecomillo` | Relay a fictional rule and its comic reversal with the source boundary needed to prevent a new listener from receiving a personal warning. | Es-05-1494 separates a quoted sentence from the speaker's assertion; here the relay must also preserve the comic reversal and prevent a fictional rule from acquiring interpersonal force. |
| `es-06-0689` | `Te desmiento` | Repair a speaker's false account of the listener's laughter while admitting the newly supplied callback as a different reading. | Es-05-1508 corrects laughter at a serious intention; here both readings are humorous, but the repair preserves their different sources instead of assigning shared episodic knowledge. |
| `es-06-0690` | `la adornes` | Correct an intermediary's invented embellishment while supplying the actual episode needed by the excluded listener. | Es-05-1495 corrects the speaker's own exaggerated quantity; this repair prevents an intermediary from manufacturing a comic ending when restoring another person's missing setup. |
| `es-06-0691` | `Te lo resumo` | Supply the missing premise of a humorous protest while respecting the listener's explicit request not to reveal the subsequent outcome. | Es-05-1480–1483 supplies shared comic context; this selective relay repairs the gap only up to the point already heard, preserving the listener's chosen limit on further information. |
| `es-06-0692` | `Descontextualicé` | Repair a professional introduction directly with its recipients by restoring the private comic performance that was transferred without its setup. | Es-05-1402 limits consent across audiences and es-06-0649 repairs personalized criticism; this repair addresses recipients whose lack of the private episode made an introduction sound like a professional evaluation. |
| `es-06-0693` | `Matizo` | Repair an overconfident intermediary interpretation by supplying the missing comic premise while leaving a possible additional criticism unresolved. | Es-05-1496 provides the speaker's confirmed meaning and es-06-0657 acts under uncertainty; this relay retracts an exclusive criticism reading because the excluded listener lacked a second, supplied comic basis. |
| `es-06-0694` | `Mezclé` | Acknowledge bringing an uninformed newcomer into a private fictional scene, restoring its setup without denying the use of her clothing. | Es-05-1488 distinguishes intended praise from its effect; this repair retains both the private fictional setup and the actual use of the newcomer's appearance instead of erasing either link. |
| `es-06-0695` | `Te completo` | Repair the referent of a humorous comparison when unsynchronized screens make two participants attach the same words to different images. | Es-05-1490 restores missing message content; this repair preserves what each participant could see at the moment of the joke and reconnects the heard comparison to its actual visual referents. |
| `es-06-0696` | `Contrapongo` | Use the restored comic episode to correct a consequential reading of a relayed announcement while retaining lack of knowledge about the actual decision. | Es-05-1506 withdraws a mistaken commitment after a tentative proposal; here an intermediary distinguishes a supplied fictional casting from an unreported real casting decision. |
| `es-06-0697` | `se lo atribuyo` | Repair a relay of collaboratively developed humor by separating the child's invented premise from the adult's later wording. | Es-05-1494 separates quotation from assertion; this repair reconstructs the relation between two comic contributions for absent recipients while preventing credit for the premise from becoming a false quotation. |
| `es-06-0698` | `te exijo` | Release an uninformed newcomer from apparent pressure to participate while explaining the private scene behind a mock demand. | Es-05-1511 clarifies inclusion in an invitation; this repair makes an unexplained play invitation recognizable and explicitly removes the participation requirement the newcomer inferred. |
| `es-06-0699` | `me adjudiques` | Reject an imagined reaction while reconstructing the supplied comic relation for someone who did not experience the episode. | Es-05-1509 distinguishes apology from forgiveness; this repair distinguishes reconstructed comic understanding from an unwarranted counterfactual claim of shared amusement. |
| `es-06-0700` | `incorporo` | Decline a proposed reuse of a comic closing by relaying the missing setup and showing how its referent would change before a new audience. | Es-05-1494 restores a quotation's source and es-06-0639 limits inferred hostility; this repair prevents reuse of a performance-dependent joke as an apparent evaluation of a new audience's real responses. |

| `es-06-0736` | `te la explico` | Reformulate the existing hospitality offer for an overhearer who treated it as an invitation to move in. | Repair both addressee and pragmatic scope without changing Ana's offer: the aunt's visit remains its occasion, and the cousin's proposed stay requires a separate request. |
